  - **DEFAULT: only on pages with the slide-over panel** (the reading page). The Subject contents page shows its list in the page on phones, and the Subjects, Search and other pages have no panel, so none of them gets the button.

- **D22. Edit mode redesign (round 7, item 2).** [Owner: "I am fine with the mock-ups that you suggested for EDIT. Execute them. One change - when editing a topic etc name, don't display the explanatory text as suggested by you - 'Topics are sorted A-Z...'."] The approved pictures are in `design/mockups/edit-mode/`. Supersedes R5-10b (icon edit bars, "+" button, quick-add), which are removed (`_edit_bar.html`, `_add_here.html`, `quick-add.js`, the old drag bar).
  - **Edit switch.** One labelled switch (pencil and "Edit") on the directory bar (`_edit_switch.html`). Off is plain reading with no controls. On gives a faint tint (`body.edit-on`) and shows Undo and Done on the bar. DEFAULT: the state is kept per browser tab in `sessionStorage` plus the server flag `?edit=1`; `edit-mode.js` adds `?edit=1` to links and redirects to it when the tab's state is on. No cookie, no database field. Only Editors get the switch; Readers never, and every new endpoint answers them 403. The switch is a link, so it works without JavaScript.
  - **Two buttons per row.** "+ Add" and "Edit" on every Topic, Sub-topic, Section, Item, Nested Item (and the Subject heading, and each Subject on the Subjects page). Small with words on a desktop; 60 by 56 px with the word under the icon on a phone (44 px or more everywhere); tooltips. Rows come from `_row_actions.html`.
  - **+ Add form** under the row (`_inline_add.html`): segmented type buttons, text with B/I/U for Items and a plain line for headings, a citation box greyed out when it does not apply, "+ another citation" with an optional comment, Save and Cancel. Enter saves and closes (it no longer opens a blank form, diary round item 1.2); Shift+Enter is a new line; Esc closes. The choices per row are in `diary/inline.py` (`add_choices`).
  - **Editing card** (`_inline_card.html`): in place on a desktop, full screen on a phone. Items: text, citations (comment, remove x, drag grip, + another citation), Flag None/Overruled/Doubted/Other with text, For review switch with note; bottom bar Save, Cancel and More (Move to..., Move up, Move down, Change to Nested Item / Item, History, Delete "goes to Trash · Undo available"). **Headings: the name and More only, with NO explanatory text** (owner instruction; a test asserts that no such text is present).
  - **Grip** at the left for Items, Sections and Nested Items only; headings have none because they are alphabetical. Dropping sends the index to `edit_move` and the page reloads so every number and version is current.
  - **Saving** uses the existing services through the existing endpoints with the header `X-Requested-With: fetch` (see FEAT-005 contract in `views_inline.py`). A stale save shows an inline message with a link that re-sends the same fields to the existing conflict screen. Items inside a Nested Item show bullets, not "(i)".
  - **Headings do not switch views while an editing card or Add form is open** (changes D20's guard).
  - **DEFAULTS taken:** (1) a heading, Section or Subject has no full-page edit form, so its Edit button needs JavaScript (an Item's Edit and every "+ Add" fall back to the existing full pages). (2) Move to... and the landing-page switch are whole pages (the card sends its fields to them as a normal post). (3) Delete in the card has no extra confirmation because Undo is on the bar. (4) The "Add a Subject" forms at the bottom of the Subjects page are kept as they were. (5) The Subject heading on the reading page also gets the two buttons (to add its first Topic or Item). (6) The Trash link is only in the program menu now; the old Undo/Trash strip at the bottom of the page is gone.

Django tests added or updated; they await CI.

- **D23. Round 8 root cause: Move to... CSRF failure (item 3).** [Owner: "I received the following error while trying to 'Move To' an item - I received it multiple times: Please try again. The security check did not pass. This usually means the page was open for a long time, cookies are blocked, or the site was opened from a different web address. Nothing was changed. Go back to the home page, sign in again if asked, and repeat what you were doing."]
  - **Cause.** `postPlain()` in `static/js/edit-mode.js` built the hidden form for the whole-page screens (Move to..., the conflict screen, Change to Nested Item when a page is needed, the landing switch) with `Array.prototype.forEach.call(new FormData(form).entries(), ...)`. `entries()` is an iterator, not an array-like, so the loop ran zero times and the form carried no fields at all, not even `csrfmiddlewaretoken`. Django correctly refused it. The server templates and CSRF settings were fine.
  - **Fix.** `new FormData(form).forEach(function (value, key) {...})`. CSRF enforcement is unchanged. `backups.views.csrf_failure` now logs the reason and path as a warning (page text unchanged) so a future failure shows in the Render logs.
  - **Tests.** `diary/tests/test_csrf_edit_mode.py` uses a client with `enforce_csrf_checks=True` (the normal test client skips CSRF, which is why this was never caught): every edit mode POST refuses a missing token and accepts the rendered one, and the whole Move to flow (card fields, picker, confirm) is replayed.

- **D24. Friendly redirects and `/caselaw` (round 8, item 1).** [Owner: "Create a ‘redirect’ 301 script, which sends anyone to the Home Page if they type the wrong page. Further, if someone types in …/caselaw, redirect them to caselaw/home (what used to be caselaw/subjects)"]
  - **Owner follow-up.** [Owner: "Just a thought, instead of calling it caselaw/home, simply redirect to 'Subjects' whenever anyone goes to /caselaw"] Decision: the page stays at `/caselaw/subjects/`. Nothing is called `caselaw/home` any more.
  - **Built.** The program index is at `/caselaw/subjects/` (URL name `diary_home`, an internal name only). `/caselaw` and `/caselaw/` answer 301 to `/caselaw/subjects/` (query string kept, so `?edit=1` survives); the old root `/subjects/` goes straight to `/caselaw/subjects/` in one hop. DEFAULT: `/caselaw/home/` (never deployed) is kept as a harmless 301 alias to `/caselaw/subjects/` rather than dropped. The page keeps its round 8 display names (title "Home - Case Law Diary", heading and breadcrumb root "Index of Subjects", Edit switch at the bottom). `subjects/<id>/` addresses are unchanged. A `handler404` (`diary/not_found.py`) answers 301 for an address that matches no route.
  - **Status 301 as requested. Caching caveat.** Browsers cache a 301 permanently, so a mistyped address that later becomes a real page keeps redirecting in a browser that already saw the redirect. A 302 would be safer; 301 is used for unmatched addresses because the owner asked for it.
  - **DEFAULT: only normal browser page requests are redirected.** GET or HEAD, `Accept` contains `text/html`, not a fetch call, not under `/static/`, not `/healthz/`, not a file-like path (.js, .css, .png, .ico, .map, .json, .txt, .xml and similar). Everything else (forms, fetch, assets, health check) gets a real 404. Forbidden pages (403) are not redirected.
  - **DEFAULT: destination.** Under `/caselaw/` and signed in with a profile: the Subjects page (`/caselaw/subjects/`). Everything else: Home (`/?home=1`, which also skips the landing-program redirect).
  - **DEFAULT: a missing record also redirects, with a message.** An address that exists but whose record is gone (deleted, or another profile's) redirects the same way and shows "That page no longer exists." on the page it lands on. The record case uses a temporary 302 (not the 301 the owner asked for), because Trash restore can make the address live again and a cached 301 would hide it; this follows the round 8 review. An address that matches nothing redirects silently with the 301.
  - **Tests.** `diary/tests/test_redirects.py`.

- **D25. Home page rename, heading and Edit switch at the bottom (round 8, item 2 a and b).** [Owner: "Rename the ‘Subjects’ page as ‘Home’. Rename the ‘Subjects’ at the top as ‘Index of Subjects’, and remove the (now-duplicated ‘Index of Subjects’ text on line 2. Move the ‘Edit’ button to the bottom of the page."]
  - **Built.** The page title is "Home - Case Law Diary"; the one heading is "Index of Subjects"; the small-caps second line and its CSS are removed. The program name link is titled "Home". The Edit switch (with Undo and Done when on) sits in a centred block at the bottom of the Home page, after Other Subjects (`.edit-switch-bottom` in `edit.css`).
  - **DEFAULT: breadcrumb root.** On reading pages the breadcrumb root, which opens the side panel's list of every Subject, now reads "Index of Subjects".
  - **DEFAULT: Edit switch placement.** It moves to the bottom on the program Home page only. On reading pages it stays on the sticky directory bar, where it is needed while scrolling. Owner question: should reading pages move it too?
  - **Not renamed.** "Subjects" where it means the records (Other Subjects, Add a Subject, Show on the Subjects page).
  - **Tests.** `diary/tests/test_views.py` (title, single heading, switch after Other Subjects, absent for Readers, breadcrumb root).

## Round 9

- **D26. Read Whole Subject (item 1).** [Owner: "When opening a Subject from the ‘Subjects page’, I receive this message: “Choose a Topic or Sub-topic from the list, or click the Subject name to read the whole Subject.” Replace it with “Read Whole Subject”, which will be a link to view the ENTIRE subject."]
  - **Built.** On the Subject contents page (`subject_contents.html`) the hint is replaced by one link, "Read Whole Subject", to `read_subject`. The side panel stays. On a phone the reading column (with the link) sits above the full-width Contents list. DEFAULT: a Subject with no Topics keeps its short sentence and gets no link (the breadcrumb leads to its Items).
- **D27. Display Name for each Profile (item 2).** [Owner: "For each Profile, allow me to add a ‘Display Name’. Instead of the name of the program, show the Display Name, on the top of the webpage."]
  - **Built.** `Profile.display_name` (optional, trimmed, up to 80 characters; migration `accounts/0014`; old backups restore it blank). It is shown in the header brand of the program pages instead of "Case Law Diary · Main" (the link still opens the program index and the aria-label follows). Blank keeps the current text. `export_name` is unchanged.
  - **DEFAULTS.** Editors of the profile change it in Profile settings (Readers only see it); the Admin changes it on the Profiles screen (Rename form, also New profile). It is also used as the title suffix on the Home and Review pages and as the text of the Home program button (blank: the program name). Owner question: should the Home button show the Display Name? Default yes.
  - **Tests.** `accounts/tests/test_display_name.py`, `backups/tests/test_backups.py`.
- **D28. Redirects (item 3, answer 2).** [Owner: "Do temporary 301 redirects"]
  - **DEFAULT interpretation.** A 301 is permanent by definition and browsers cache it, so this is read as TEMPORARY redirects, HTTP 302. An address that matches no route now answers 302 to Home (or to the Index of Subjects inside /caselaw/ with a profile). Missing records were already 302. The deliberate permanent redirects for renamed addresses stay 301: /caselaw to /caselaw/subjects/, the /caselaw/home/ alias, and the old root-level diary addresses. Owner question: should those also be temporary? Default no.
- **D29. Edit button redesign (item 3, answers 1 and 3).** [Owner: "(1) Yes (2) Do temporary 301 redirects (3) Yes." to: "Which Edit button design do you want? (A, with C’s strip.)", "Keep the 301 ...?", "Should Edit stay in the top bar on reading pages, with only the Index of Subjects page having it at the bottom? (Yes.)"]
  - **Built.** Mockups A and C (`design/mockups/edit-button/`). Off: a quiet "Edit" link with a pencil. On: a dark-red pill "Editing · Done" (press to finish). While editing, a slim dark-red strip is stuck across the very top: "Editing · Undo · Done" (`_edit_strip.html`, `.edit-strip` in `edit.css`); the breadcrumb row sticks below it. The old switch track and the separate Undo and Done buttons on the bar are gone. Behaviour is unchanged (per-tab state, Editors only, server refuses Readers). Placement is unchanged: top bar on reading pages, bottom of the Index of Subjects page.
  - **Tests.** `diary/tests/test_views_edit.py`.

- **D30. My settings: opening page and number formats (Milestone 4d).** The **My settings** page (every signed-in user, Readers included) now has two sections besides the existing "Open after login" choice. (1) **Page inside the case law diary**, used when the Case Law Diary is the chosen program: Subjects (Index of Subjects), Global Search, the last page I viewed, or a chosen Subject (the Subject dropdown shows only for that option). A stored legacy value `other_subjects` still opens `/caselaw/subjects/#other-subjects` but is no longer offered and is shown as Subjects. (2) **Number formats** for Topics, Sub-topics, Sections, Items, Items inside a Nested Item and citations of an Item with several, with a **Preview** rendered by the server (`diary/preview.py`, pure Python, the same numbering functions and full stop rule as the reading page) and fetched live by `static/js/my-settings.js` from `/my-settings/preview/`; without JavaScript it updates after Save. "Use the standard formats" only resets the dropdowns. A field that is not posted is left unchanged, a posted bad value is an error, and users without a diary profile get no diary section. Changing formats never touches stored data.
  - **Nested Item children.** `UserSettings.nested_item_format` now means the format of the Items inside a Nested Item (default `bullet`; migration `0012` changes the default and turns the never-used stored `paren_decimal` into `bullet`). The Nested Item itself stays in the shared Items run.
  - **Last page.** `accounts.User.last_diary_path` (migration `accounts/0015`) is written with a queryset `update` only when it changes, after a GET that returned 200 on the Subjects index, Subject contents, reading pages, Search and the Review list; never for `?edit=1`, POST, errors or addresses over 500 characters. On use it must start with `/caselaw/`, resolve to one of those pages and, for a Subject, Topic or Sub-topic, still be live in the user's profile; otherwise the Subjects index opens.
  - **Home stays reachable.** The opening page applies at login and on the bare `/` entry visit only; `?home=1` and in-site navigation still reach Home, and a safe `?next` still wins at login.
  - **Tests.** `diary/tests/test_preview.py` and `test_numbering.py` (pure), `diary/tests/test_my_settings.py`, `backups/tests/test_backups.py`.

## Milestone 4: Search, Review list, My settings

- **D31. Milestone 4 choices (built by the assistant; each is a default the owner can change).**
  - **Search technique.** Python-side normalised matching instead of normalised columns and pg_trgm (section 10). Deviation from the earlier plan in section 10 and PLAN.md. Reason: nothing to keep in step, no extension, no migration risk, fully testable. Upgrade path in section 10.
  - **Results per page.** 50, "Show more" adds 50, up to 1,000.
  - **Ranking.** All words together in one field first, then diary order.
  - **Opening page merge.** The earlier "Opening page" idea is merged with the existing "Open after login" program choice (D30): one place, two steps. "Other Subjects" is no longer a separate opening page.
  - **Last page storage.** One column on the user (`User.last_diary_path`), updated only when it changes, validated on use (D30). Stored on the server, so it follows the person to another device (unlike the Contents "last view", which is remembered per browser).
  - **`nested_item_format` reinterpreted.** It was never used; it now sets the format of the Items inside a Nested Item (default bullet), and a stored `paren_decimal` was changed to `bullet` by migration `diary/0012`. This changes the number shown only if a user had chosen a non-default value, which no screen allowed.
  - **Header search box.** Added to every diary page except Search and the Index of Subjects page. Owner question below.
  - **Jump flash.** A 2.5 second highlight of the target when a link ends in `#n-...`, with an outline instead of a fade for reduced motion.
  - **Review list.** Rebuilt on the search code and extended to Items inside Nested Items (a change in behaviour: they were not listed before).
- **D32. Custom Name and Date format are not part of Milestone 4.** The Milestone 4 deliverable in the plan listed them, but they only have a purpose once files are exported, so they are moved to Milestone 6 (Word export), together with the Time zone for file names. My settings in Milestone 4 has no Custom Name, Date format or Time zone screen. Default; the owner can ask for them earlier (they are small, and could be built without export, only with a preview).
- **Questions for the owner (Milestone 4), each with its default.**
  1. Results per page: 50, with "Show more"? *Default: 50.*
  2. Ranking: all words together first, then diary order? *Default: yes.* Alternative: strictly diary order, or citations before comments.
  3. Should "&" stay different from "and" in search? *Default: yes (I6).*
  4. Build Custom Name, Date format and Time zone in Milestone 6 with the export? *Default: yes.*
  5. Should the header search box show on every page? *Default: yes except the Search page and the Index of Subjects page.*
  6. Should the "last page viewed" be remembered on the server (follows you to another device)? *Default: yes.*
  7. Should the Review list include Items inside Nested Items? *Default: yes.*
  8. Is it fine that a search for a word in a Note opens its parent Item rather than a Note page? *Default: yes (Notes arrive properly in Milestone 5).*
- **Tests.** `diary/tests/test_searchtext.py` (pure; 42 tests, with 45 real citations from the Word file), `test_search.py`, `test_views_search.py`, `test_preview.py`, `test_my_settings.py`, review tests in `test_importer_db.py`. The pure ones pass locally; the Django ones have not been run (Django cannot be installed in the build environment), so GitHub CI is their first run.

## Milestone 5: Notes and cross-references

- **D33. Note editing is a full page, not an inline card.** A Note has a text box and a list of links, which does not fit the small inline card. Its Edit button (Edit mode only) opens `/edit/note/<id>/`: rich text B/I/U, the links (Up, Down, Change, Remove, "Add a link..."), and More (Move to..., Move up/down, History, Delete). On a phone the page is the whole screen with a Save bar fixed at the bottom, and every button is at least 44px. Every button on the page posts the whole form, so typed text is saved first in the same Undo step. Default; the owner can ask for an inline card instead.
- **D34. "+ Add" offers Note on every row** (Subject, Topic, Sub-topic, Section, Item, Nested Item and an Item inside one) as "inside:note". It goes last among the Notes of that parent. The inline form takes the text only; the editor page opens for links (without JavaScript the add redirects there).
- **D35. Display.** Each link is "See also: Subject > Topic > ..." with a link icon, going to the target in the whole-Subject page at its anchor (`scroll-margin` keeps it clear of the sticky bar). The target (heading, Section or Item) shows one line "Linked from: <path>" per live incoming Note, each going back to the Note. A target in Trash, or below something in Trash, shows "(target deleted)" and is not clickable; the link row is never touched, so it works again when the target is restored. A Note in Trash (or below something in Trash) shows no "Linked from" at the other end.
- **D36. Order and search.** Notes come after the Items and Sections of their parent and before child headings (D5). They can be reordered by hand among the Notes of the same parent (Move up/down, D18). Links of a Note have their own order (new column `NoteLink.position`, migration 0013, existing links numbered in creation order). A search hit on a Note opens `#n-note-<id>`.
- **D37. Picker.** The tree picker for links and for Move to on a Note drills down (All Subjects, then a Subject, Topic, Sub-topic or Nested Item) and lists the Items and Sections inside, each with a Choose button; the place being viewed can also be chosen. Only live records of the same profile are offered, and the server refuses anything else (another profile's record is "not found").
- **D38. Trash, History, Undo.** A removed link goes to the Trash (Link to ...), can be restored and Undone. Deleting a Note takes its links with it; restoring the Note brings them back in order. Every link change writes History (create, edit for a change of target, reorder, delete, restore).
- **D39. Permanent delete.** Refused while any link (live, or in Trash) points at the record; the refusal screen lists each link with "Point it somewhere else..." (retarget) and "Remove this link for good" (Admin only; the link's History is kept). After that the delete can be repeated.
- **D40. Readers** see Notes, See also and Linked from, never an Edit button; every Note address and POST gives 403 to a Reader.
- **D41. Owner exclusions for the remaining milestones.** Milestone 6: **no Excel copy**. Milestone 7: **no split screen** (sections 5.9 and 12 and the plan updated).

**Defaults the owner can change (Milestone 5):** D33 (full page editor), D34 (a Note needs text when added), D37 (drill-down picker), D39 (remove-for-good is Admin only).

## Milestone 6: Word export and round-trip check

**Owner exclusion (D41): no Excel copy.** The machine-readable Backup button on the Backups screen stays the only backup format.

- **D42. How the .docx is written: the standard library only.** `diary/wordexport/docxwriter.py` builds the WordprocessingML by hand with `zipfile`; `python-docx` is **not** a dependency (`requirements.txt` is unchanged). Reasons: the project's importer is already a standard-library reader, so the export can be re-read by the same reader in tests and in the round-trip check; the export needs exact control of styles, literal numbers, hanging indents and one Word section (with its own page header) per Subject; and `python-docx` could not be installed or run where this was written, so code using it would have been untested until CI. Output is deterministic (fixed zip time stamps). Parts: `[Content_Types].xml`, `_rels`, `word/document.xml`, `styles.xml`, one `headerN.xml` per section, `footer1.xml` (Page N), `docProps/core.xml`. Not checked in real Word here (no Word or LibreOffice in the build environment): element order follows the OOXML schema, but the first open in Word is on your check list.
- **D43. Numbers are literal text.** Topic, Sub-topic, Section, Item and bullet labels come from `diary/numbering.py` with your My settings formats at the moment of export. Each line is `label`, a tab, then the text, with a hanging indent of 1 cm, so the file matches the screen exactly and survives editing in Word. **No full stops** are added after numbers (the answer to the round 4 question 1 is taken as the default "no"). Items restart at (1) after each Section because the labels are computed, not because of Word lists. A format of "None" leaves the label empty but keeps the tab.
- **D44. Contents page.** A plain list of Subjects (the Other Subjects group with its Subjects below it), each a clickable link to a bookmark on its heading, not a Word TOC field. A TOC field shows nothing useful until Word updates it, asks a question on opening, and cannot list the Other Subjects at Heading 2 without also listing every Topic. A document with a single Subject, and a Current View, also lists the Topics (or Sub-topics) one level down. There are no page numbers; the Navigation pane of Word works on the Heading styles.
- **D45. Layout.** Front page: the export name (Title), the part (Subtitle), for a Current View "On screen: Subject > Topic > ...", "Exported <date>. Records in this document: N Items.", "Review markers: included / not included", then Contents. Every Subject (and, in the Other Subjects group, every Other Subject) is a new Word section: it starts on a new page and has its own page header (the Subject's name; for a Current View the whole on-screen path; for an Other Subject "Other Subjects: <name>"). The "Other Subjects" Heading 1 has its own page listing its Subjects. Styles: Heading 1 (Subject), Heading 2 (Topic; an Other Subject in the group), Heading 3 (a Topic of an Other Subject, which the import never creates), `DiarySubtopic` (bold numbered line), `DiarySection` (bold, numbered), `DiaryItem`, `DiaryBullet` (children of a Nested Item), `DiaryNote`, `DiarySeeAlso`. Items directly under a heading come first, then its Notes, then its child headings. A Current View of a Subject is Heading 1 with Topics at Heading 2 (a single Other Subject: Heading 1 alone, D3); a Topic or Sub-topic view is Heading 1 with its on-screen letter or number kept.
- **D46. The record count is the number of Items at every depth** (Nested Items and the Items inside them count), the same figure the Subjects page shows beside each Subject (`item_counts_by_subject`). `manifest.txt` lists each file with its count and the total, which equals the Full export's count (a test checks this on the real file).
- **D47. What a line contains.** The Item text keeps b, i and u. Then ` - ` and the citation in italics; several citations are ` - (a) <citation> <comment> ; (b) <citation> <comment>` (labels from the "Citations of an Item with several" format, the separator ` ; ` as on screen); a Nested Item shows its own citation. A **flag** (Overruled, Doubted, Other or its text) is added as ` [Overruled]` in **red text with the flag word** (a Word file may be printed in black, so the word carries the meaning). A **Note** prints its text, then one line `See also: Subject > Topic > ...` per link, with ` (target deleted)` for a target in Trash. **"Linked from" lines are not printed** (default). **Review markers** are printed only if "Include review markers" is ticked, as amber ` [For review: <note>]` at the end of the Item or Section line.
- **D48. Who may export.** Readers: Current View only. Full (one file) and Full by Subject (.zip): Editors of the profile and the Admin (an Admin may export in full even if their own membership role is Reader; the other Admin rights are unchanged). Everything is limited to the user's own profile and excludes Trash; another profile's Subject, Topic or Sub-topic number gives "not found". The **Export** link is in the program navigation for everyone with a profile (a Reader needs it for a Current View; the Full buttons are simply not shown to a Reader), and **Export this view** sits beside the breadcrumb on the Subject page and the reading pages (Subject, Topic, Sub-topic), for everyone. It is not offered on Search, Review, Trash or History.
- **D49. File names and the date.** `[export name] [part] [date].docx`. The export name is the profile's (`Profile.export_name`, default "Case Law Diary <profile>"); it replaces the per-user Custom Name of D11 (profile answer 8), so there is no personal Custom Name setting. **Illegal characters are replaced with a hyphen** (`accounts.profiles.safe_file_name` used to remove them; it now replaces them, and the Profile settings preview says which were replaced). Over-long Current View names are shortened by the documented rule (each segment cut to 30 characters at a word boundary after the 15th, then the middle segments replaced by one `-`), and the full path is always inside the document. Two files in one zip with the same cleaned name get ` (2)`, ` (3)` before the date. **Date format:** chosen in **My settings** ("Date in Word file names", per user, the existing `UserSettings.export_date_format` column, so no migration), not in Profile settings as first suggested, because the column already exists per user and a profile-wide date format would let one person change another's file names; default `YYYY-MM-DD`. **Time zone:** India (Asia/Kolkata, IST), taken from `UserSettings.time_zone` (default Asia/Kolkata, not editable on screen); if the server has no time zone database a fixed +05:30 is used. A file made late in the evening UTC carries the Indian date.
- **D50. Speed and memory.** The whole profile is read in 8 fixed queries (not one per Subject or Item), walked in Python, rendered in memory and sent in one response. Measured: building the Word file for the real file's 950 Items takes about 0.1 s and 5,000 generated Items about 0.3 s (pure code, locally); the database part is measured in CI by `PerformanceTests` in `diary/tests/test_wordexport_db.py` (it prints the seconds for 5,000 Items, Full and zip, and fails above 30 s, half of Render's 60 s limit). 5,000 Items need a few tens of megabytes. No background job is needed; if a profile ever grew to tens of thousands of Items, the export would move to a background job.
- **D51. Round-trip check.** `python manage.py check_export_roundtrip --profile NAME [--file "Case Law Diary.docx"] [--output report.txt]` exports the profile in full (standard number formats, no review markers), reads both files with `diary/importer/docx_reader.py`, classifies the original with the importer's own plan, and checks that every Item and Section line of the original appears **exactly once**, under the same Subject, Topic and Sub-topic, in the same order, with the text exactly as in Word and each citation in italics at the end of the line. It lists the **deliberate differences** (alphabetical order of headings, literal numbers in your formats, 19 trailing colons removed, Sections numbered, "Miscellaneous" now "Other Subjects", citations in italics after " - ") and fails (exit code 1) on anything else. The code is `diary/wordexport/roundtrip.py` (pure Python). Result on the real file, locally through the pure path (plan to export to check): 950 of 950 Item lines, 2 of 2 Section lines, 693 of 693 citations in italics, 0 unexpected differences. CI repeats it through the database (import into a fresh profile, export, compare).
- **D52. A small fix found on the way.** The reading page ordered a Note's links by creation, ignoring the order set in the Note editor (Up and Down). It now orders by position, as the editor and the export do.

**Questions for the owner (Milestone 6), each with its default:**
1. Numbers as literal text instead of Word auto-numbering (D43). *Default: yes.*
2. Full stops after numbers in Word? *Default: no.*
3. The date format is a personal choice in My settings and the time zone is fixed to India (D49). *Default: yes.*
4. A plain contents list instead of a Word TOC field (D44). *Default: yes.*
5. Flags are red text plus the word (D47); "Linked from" is not printed. *Default: yes.*
6. The Admin may do the Full exports even when their own role in the profile is Reader (D48). *Default: yes.*

## Milestone 7 final decisions (performance and rehearsal)

- **D53. Performance pass, no change in behaviour.** The whole-Subject reading page already loads a Subject in a fixed number of queries. The remaining per-row queries were in "Linked from" (each incoming link followed its Note and the Note's parents one query at a time) and in a Note's own links (each link followed its target). Both now load those parents in the same query with `select_related` (`diary/reading.py`), so the count no longer depends on how many Items or links there are. Search and the Review list read plain values in bulk and need no change; the Word export reads the whole profile in bulk (D50) and only follows a link to a record in Trash one at a time (rare). `diary/tests/test_query_counts.py` fails if the whole-Subject page (about 200 Items), the Review list or Search needs more than 40 queries, or more than 2 queries extra for 10 times the data. No cache, no extra service: memory use is unchanged.
- **D54. Backup rehearsal command.** `python manage.py backup_rehearsal` takes a backup (kind "manual"), reads it back with the same checks as an upload, and compares the number of records per record type in the file with the live database. It prints a table, ends with "Backup rehearsal OK", and exits with an error if any count differs or the file cannot be read. It never restores and never changes diary data. The restore of an old-format file (users with a role, no profiles) is covered by tests in `backups/tests/test_rehearsal.py`.
- **D55. Program feature-complete.** After Milestone 7 no further milestone is planned. The owner exclusions stand: no split screen and no Excel copy (D41).

**Questions for the owner (Milestone 7), each with its default:**
1. The off-site copy stays optional and off until you set it up. *Default: yes.*
2. The rehearsal command stores one extra "manual" backup each time it runs. *Default: yes (old ones are removed by the normal retention).*

## Round 10 (part 1)

- **D56. Edit history is kept for 30 days.** [Owner: "With respect to edit history, perhaps I can only keep edit history for, say, 1 month?"] **Decision (default for the details, pending owner confirmation):** this replaces D7 and the Milestone 3 "keep all history" rule, and the original specification line "at least 20 versions". Every night, after the backup has succeeded (the same safety rule as the deleted-profile purge: it refuses if today's backup is missing or failed), `python manage.py purge_old_history` removes version rows whose change is older than `HISTORY_RETENTION_DAYS` (default 30, set in `render.yaml`). It always keeps (a) the newest version of every record, so the History page and Restore never lose the current state, and (b) every row that shares an Undo group with a row inside the window, so a recent Undo still works. The count removed is written to the Permanent removal log (a `PurgeLog` row). The History page says "Edit history is kept for 30 days" (the number follows the setting). Versions older than the window cannot be viewed or restored, but they remain in the nightly backups for their 30-plus days of retention.
- **D56 open question for the owner.** Should the 30 days be counted from the change itself (what is built), or should the app also keep a minimum number of versions per record whatever their age? *Default: counted from the change, no minimum (apart from the newest version).*
- **D57. Efficiency settings.** WhiteNoise hashed static file names, Gunicorn 2 workers x 2 threads recycled every 500 requests, and `clearsessions` in the nightly job (a failure there never hides a backup failure). See docs/HOSTING_AND_DEPLOY.md section 5a.

- **D58. Nested Items are removed.** [Owner (round 10, item 3): "I am not able to change ‘Nested Items’ back into multi-citation items. I am getting an error asking me “Choose what happens to the Items beneath it: keep them as Items or fold them into Cases.” I think, the Nested Item concept is a mistake. Instead, let it simply be a design choice. So, remove the concept of ‘Nested Items’ all existing ‘Nested Items’ will be converted into regular Items, with multiple citations. The text for each sub-item in a nested item will simply be the ‘Comment’ alongside each citation. In edit, for multi-citation items, I will simply be given a choice as to whether I want to view the citations inline (which is the default), or in separate lines (similar to how nested items were to be, but with the same a, b, c… numbering as inline multiple citations, and, the ‘comment’ listed before the citation itself)"] **Decision:** supersedes D4, section 6 (conversions), M3-10, M3-19 and the Nested Item parts of 10d, D30 and D34. `Item.parent_item`, the kind "nested", the Nested Item add choice (Add form: Item | Section only; quick-add and add-after likewise), the "Change to" buttons and screen, the "More" menu entry, Items as "Move to" targets, and the `nested_item_format` setting (My settings and its preview) are all gone (migration 0016). `Item.kind` stays as a column that can only be `plain`. The importer is unchanged (your file never had a Nested Item): 41 Subjects, 178 Topics, 323 Sub-topics, 2 Sections, 950 Items (691 clean, 259 flagged), 261 review-marked, 693 citations.
- **D58a. Re-ordering citations (round 10, item 2).** [Owner: "Multi-citation items - I am not able to re-order the citations."] **Cause and fix:** the editing card moved a row by dragging with the pointer captured on the grip; moving the row in the page releases the capture after the first step, so a drag could not finish. The drag now listens on the document. Each citation row in the card also has **Up and Down buttons** (keyboard and touch), like the Item editor page already had. The server saves the order exactly as posted, in one Undo step; tests post a swap and a three-way rotation through a client that enforces the CSRF token and check the positions and the single Undo group.
- **D59. Citations on separate lines.** Part of the owner's words above. **Decision (defaults for the details, pending owner confirmation):** `Item.citation_layout` is `inline` (default) or `lines`. The "Show citations" choice appears in the editing card and on the Item editor page only when the Item has more than one citation (it appears as soon as a second row is added). Lines look like "(a) <comment> - *<citation>*" with the same label format as inline (the citation-letter format of My settings), hanging indent, comment first; no comment: "(a) *<citation>*"; no citation: the comment only. One citation always shows inline whatever the choice. The Word export prints the same lines as literal text (style "DiaryBullet", renamed in code to the citation line style) and the round-trip check still compares 950 of 950 Items because it ignores those lines.
- **D60. How the old Nested Items were converted (migrations 0014 to 0016; `diary/nested_conversion.py`).** **Decision (defaults, pending owner confirmation):** the Nested Item N keeps its id, flag, review marker, Notes, links and history, and becomes a plain Item whose citations are Cases, shown on separate lines. (1) N's own citation becomes Case (a) with no comment. (2) Each sub-item in order: its own Cases move to N (same rows) and the sub-item's text is put in front of the first one's comment, "<text> - <comment>", paragraphs kept; a sub-item with no citation becomes a Case with an empty citation and the text as comment. (3) A sub-item's flag and For-review marker are written in square brackets after that comment, "[Overruled: reason]", "[For review: note]", so nothing disappears. (4) The sub-item's Notes move to N and Note links to it now point at N. (5) N is marked For review with a note saying what was folded, so you can look at each one. (6) The sub-items are not deleted: they go to Trash in a Trash batch labelled "Nested Item conversion: ...", where they can be read. A sub-item that was already in Trash stays there on its own. (7) Every changed record gets a "migrate" history row. **Safety:** all in one transaction; record counts (Items, Notes, links, Cases created) are checked before and after and any mismatch rolls everything back; the data move is a separate migration (0015) from the schema changes (0014 before, 0016 after); the reverse of 0015 is refused while any Item or Case exists (restore the "pre_migration" backup the update takes first). The same code maps an **old backup file** when it is restored (a file with Nested Items is converted before anything is written, with its Notes and links), and an **old version in History** (a Nested Item's citation becomes Case (a) on restore).
- **D60 open question for the owner.** Is it right that the text of a sub-item that has several citations goes only on the first of them? *Default: yes.* Should the "For review" mark stay on each converted Item until you clear it? *Default: yes, so each can be checked.*
- **D61. Items live in a Topic called "1" when they have none.** [Owner (round 10, item 6): "Any items which are not housed in a Topic will automatically be housed in a topic called “1”"] **Decision (defaults for the details, pending owner confirmation):** an Item or Section that is added to (the Add form, quick "+ Add", add-after), moved to ("Move to", dragging) or restored from Trash under a Subject goes into that Subject's Topic named "1" instead. The Topic is made on demand, as part of the same Undo step, and reused if it exists (a live Topic called "1" in any letter case). Topics are always alphabetical, so "1" sorts first and takes the letter A. **Data migration 0017** did the same once for the stored data: every Item and Section directly under a Subject moved into Topic "1" in the same order (after anything already in it); records in Trash move too and keep their trash state; a Subject in Trash gets a Topic "1" in Trash with it; every moved record has a "migrate" history row and a version bump; counts are checked inside the transaction and any mismatch rolls it back; the reverse is refused while any Item or Section exists. An old backup file is mapped the same way when restored (`diary/topic_one.py`). **Left as it is:** a Subject that holds Sub-topics directly (the Other Subjects) cannot also hold Topics (section 2.1), so its Items stay directly under it. In the real Word file (checked with the import plan, no database): 17 Items sit directly under 9 Subjects, none of them Subjects with Sub-topics, so all 17 are housed (in 9 new Topics "1"); 22 Subjects hold Sub-topics directly and have no loose Item. Notes are not moved: a Note on a Subject is a note about the Subject itself. A fresh Word import still puts Items exactly where the file does (the import figures are unchanged); run `python manage.py house_loose_items` after such an import, and the import reconciliation keeps matching because it counts a housed Item as sitting under its Subject. There is no special display for Topic "1": it is an ordinary Topic.
- **D61 open questions for the owner.** (1) Should Notes that sit on a Subject also go into Topic "1"? *Default: no, they stay on the Subject.* (2) Should the Word import house the loose Items itself? *Default: no, so the import stays a faithful copy of the file; use the command.* (3) The Subjects that hold Sub-topics directly keep loose Items under the Subject. *Default: none exist in your file.*

## Round 11 (part 2)

- **D62. Home icon at the far right; Log out only on suite Home.** [Owner (round 11, item 1): "Move the ‘Home’ button to the absolute right, where ‘Log Out’ currently is. ONLY show log out in the Main / Home page, and do not show the home button in that page."] **Decision:** the house icon (44px, label "Home", tooltip "Home (⌥H)", link `/?home=1`) is now the last item in the header on every page except the suite Home page. Log out appears only on the suite Home page (the program picker) and the Home icon does not. Back and Forward stay to the right of the program or page name. **Defaults (pending owner confirmation):** the other suite pages (Users, Profiles, Settings, My settings, Backups, Import, Help) show the Home icon at the right and **no** Log out, so Log out is reachable only from Home; the user name stays shown on the suite pages that had it. This reverses R5-2 (icon at the left of the name).
- **D63. Search is an icon that opens a bar; shortcuts shown as on a Mac.** [Owner (round 11, item 7): "Perhaps, instead of a search box, have a search symbol, and when I press it, a search bar pops up. Instead of Option-F, let it be Option-G. Display all shortcuts as for Mac."] **Decision:** program pages have a magnifier icon button (44px, label "Search", tooltip "Search (⌥G)") in place of the header search box; it opens a bar under the header with the input (focused), a Search button and a link to the full Search page. Enter submits to `/caselaw/search/?q=`; Esc or a click outside closes it (Esc returns focus to the icon); on phones the bar is full width. The big box on the Index of Subjects page is removed for the same reason. The Search page keeps its own box, and ⌥G focuses it. **Shortcuts:** the keys are unchanged in code (Alt+G, Alt+H, and Ctrl+Alt+G and Ctrl+Alt+H still work because the browser reports the same key), but every place that names them (tooltips, Help, README, this log) shows ⌥G Search and ⌥H Home. **Defaults:** G was already the Search key (the owner's "Option-F" is taken as a slip); Windows users are told on the Help page that Alt is Option.
- **D64. Export button and Export page cleanup.** [Owner (round 11, item 4): "Change the button for ‘Export this view’ to simply ‘Export’. Give me mockups for alternative options too. Further, in the page where you get to select what to export, rename ‘A whole Subject’ to ‘Subject’, and delete the ‘subject’ on the next line above the box. No need for it to be mentioned twice. Similarly, only put one box for ‘Include Review Markers’, without the text in the bracket saying “(THE AMBER… SECTIONS)”, which will cover all selections. Let Include Review Markers be checked by default. Delete the language for ‘File names’."] **Decision:** the button on Subject, Topic and Sub-topic pages reads "Export". **Default:** the top-bar link and this button share the label "Export" (both open the Export page). The Export page has "Subject" as the heading with the picker below it (its label is visually hidden, so the word is read once), one "Include review markers" box with no bracketed text that applies to every download (the Subject picker is now in the same form), checked by default, and no File names section or file-name lines (naming is unchanged and still set in Profile settings and My settings). **This reverses the earlier default** of leaving review markers out. **Deviation:** the default lives in the Export page (opened with `review=0` it is unticked); the export services still default to no markers when called without the argument, so a direct download URL without `review=1` omits them, as the unticked box does. Mockups of five alternatives: `design/mockups/round-11/export-button/` (recommendation: A).
- **D65. Move to a new Topic or Sub-topic.** [Owner (round 11, item 5): "In ‘Move To’, also provide the option to Move to New Topic in same Subject, and move to new Sub-Topic in same Topic."] **Decision:** at the top of the Move to picker there are two choices, "New Topic in this Subject" and "New Sub-topic in this Topic". You are asked for the name, then shown the usual list of numbers that change, then the new heading is made and the record moved into it as **one Undo step**. **Rules (defaults, pending owner confirmation):** Items and Sections are offered both; a Sub-topic is offered only the new Topic; a Topic is offered neither. "New Topic" is hidden when the Subject holds Sub-topics directly (a Subject holds Topics or Sub-topics, not both, section 2.1). "New Sub-topic" is shown only when the record sits in a Topic (directly, or through a Sub-topic of that Topic). The name is required and must differ, ignoring capitals, from the live siblings (the Topics of that Subject, or the Sub-topics of that Topic); otherwise the name form is shown again with the reason. The new heading takes its alphabetical place, so other Topic letters may change and are listed in the preview. Readers get 403. Code: `diary/services/moves.py` (`move_to_new_heading`), `views_edit.move_new`.
- **D66. Search results: suggestions only.** [Owner (round 11, item 8): "The Search answers need some work, to be a bit more readable. Give me suggestions."] **Decision:** no change to the live Search page. Three layouts (cards, grouped by Subject and Topic, two panes) are mocked up with real lines from the Word file in `design/mockups/round-11/search-results/`, with a README listing pros and cons, a recommendation (cards, with folding groups for long result lists) and other readability suggestions. Waiting for the owner to choose.
- **D67. The Nested Item conversion must leave no Item beneath another Item, whatever the shape.** [Owner (hotfix after round 11): "I am not able to deploy. I receive the following error in the log: diary.legacy_merge.MigrationVerificationError: The Nested Item conversion was stopped and rolled back: 7 Items still sit beneath another Item. You may note that I currently have a few nested lists."] **Root cause:** migration 0015 visited only Items whose own `kind` is "nested" and converted only their direct children. Its check, correctly, demands that no Item row (live or in Trash) still has `parent_item` set. Any Item beneath a parent that is not a Nested Item (the database never required the parent to be nested; only the child must be plain) or beneath an inner group was never visited, so 7 rows were left and the check stopped the migration. It rolled back, so the live data is untouched. The exact shape of the owner's 7 rows cannot be seen from the code; the new `nested_report` command shows them. **Decision:** (a) every Item that has Items beneath it is converted, of any kind and at any depth, deepest group first, so each parent absorbs finished children (`conversion_order`); (b) a child takes its place (Subject, Topic or Sub-topic) from the nearest ancestor that has one; (c) live children of a live parent are folded as before (text becomes the comment of the first citation, Notes and links move to the parent, parent marked For review, children to a Trash batch "Nested Item conversion"); children already in Trash stay in Trash, detached with the parent's place so restoring them still works; (d) a final sweep detaches anything still attached (for example a cycle), keeping its state, with a "migrate" history row; (e) the check is unchanged (no Item row has a parent, counts reconcile). The old-backup loader uses the same order, place and bookkeeping, so a backup and the live database map alike. `python manage.py nested_report` lists, read-only and on the old schema too, every Item beneath another Item and what will happen to it. Steps for the owner are in docs/HOSTING_AND_DEPLOY.md section 5b.
- **D68. Adding on a phone.** [Owner: "Further, I am not able to add a new item in Mobile mode. I am told to go to desktop mode."] **Finding:** the round 11 code contains no such message and no desktop test: since round 7 "+ Add" is a link to the full Add page (`?after=item:5` or `?parent=topic:3`) that edit-mode.js turns into the inline form on every screen size, with the full-screen card on phones. The "desktop only" behaviour was round 5's quick-add, removed in round 7. The deployed site is still the older version while the deploy fails, which is the likely source of the message. **Decision:** no behaviour change; tests now pin it (the button is a real link with the place, the Add page works for a phone browser under the real CSRF check, and the scripts never test for a wide screen or a mouse). Owner question: please check again after the deploy succeeds and tell me the exact wording and page if it still appears.

## Round 12

- **D69. Add on the phone matches the desktop; Flag and For review on both Add forms.** [Owner (round 12, item 1): "Let the ‘+Add’ page on the phone have the same types as the desktop form. Further, the Flag & For Review Options  have disappeared when adding a new Item, but are there when I edit the item. I want them there on both."] **Decision:** the full Add page lists exactly what `inline.add_choices` gives for the row you pressed "+ Add" on (one shared function, so the two cannot drift): for example after an Item or Section, Item, Section and Note; on a Topic, Item inside, Sub-topic, Note and Topic. The page posts `ref` and `choice` like the inline form, with the version check. Both forms show **Flag** (None, Overruled, Doubted, Other with a short text) and **For review** (switch and optional note) when the type is Item, **For review** only when it is a Section, and neither for a Note or a heading (hidden and disabled; the server also ignores them). They are saved by the same create service in the same Undo step as the add, and checked as when editing (the flag must be one of the choices, flag text at most 300 characters). **Defaults (pending owner confirmation):** the Flag starts at None and For review off. A review note is kept only when For review is on. Questions: should a Note also be markable? *Default: no.*
- **D70. Move to is a collapsed tree.** [Owner (round 12, item 7): "In the ‘Move To’ page, it is hard to navigate through the various subjects / topics / sub-topics. Add arrows which allow them to expand / contract, and automatically start with everything contracted."] **Decision:** every Subject and Topic that holds something has an arrow button (44px, keyboard focusable, `aria-expanded`) and the whole tree starts closed. **Expand all** and **Collapse all** buttons sit above the tree, like the Contents list. "New Topic in this Subject" and "New Sub-topic in this Topic" stay visible at the top. The Subject the record sits in now is marked "current" (**default:** it is marked but not opened, since the owner asked for everything closed). Without JavaScript the arrows and buttons are hidden and the whole tree is shown open. Open/closed choices are not remembered between visits (**default**). Code: `views_edit.picker_tree`, `templates/diary/_picker_nodes.html`, `static/js/site.js`.
- **D71. Search: By Subject view, Search headers, Option+F, icon only.** [Owner (round 12, item 2): "Modify the search feature as follows: when searching limited to a specific subject, use Option A. When searching the entire database, provide an option to view subject-wise - this will display a version which looks like Option C with a fundamental difference. The column on the left will show a list of subjects in which search items appear. When you click on any of those subjects, the list of search items within that subject will display on the left. Remove the tab for ‘Review List’ in Search." Item 3: "Let the shortcut for search be Option + F, not Option + G." Item 4: "Remove the word ‘search’ in the top bar. Instead, only keep the Magnifying glass button, and let it be persistent, as it anyway lets you go to the full search page." Item 6 (second sentence): "In ’Search’, also keep a toggle for ‘Search Headers’, which ONLY searches within the Subject, Topic, Sub-Topic and Section names."] **Decision:** (2a) with a Subject chosen, results are Option A cards. (2b) With no Subject chosen the default is also cards, with a "View: All results | By Subject" switch. By Subject (`?view=subjects&s=<id>`) shows a narrow left column of the Subjects that have hits, with counts, in Subject order (main Subjects, then Other Subjects), and the chosen Subject's results as cards in the main pane (the first Subject when none is chosen; "Show more" per Subject beyond 50). On a phone the Subject list comes first; a tap shows that Subject's results with an "All Subjects" back link. (2c) The Review list link inside Search is gone; the For review only filter and the Review menu entry stay. (6b) "Search headers" (`?headers=1`) searches only Subject, Topic, Sub-topic and Section names (not Item text, citations, comments, flags, review notes or Notes); it combines with the Subject filter, never reaches Trash or another profile. (3) The shortcut is Option+F (Alt+F, and Ctrl+Alt+F, on Windows); Alt+H is unchanged. It is ignored while typing in a text field, so it never steals a keystroke. Everywhere that named Option+G now says Option+F (older entries above are kept as history). (4) The word "Search" is removed from the top bar; the magnifier stays on every diary page, including the Search page (where it focuses the page's box) and the Index of Subjects. The pop-up keeps its "Full Search page" link. **Defaults (pending owner confirmation):** cards stay the default view; By Subject is not remembered between visits; Search headers with Citations only finds nothing (headers have no citations). Windows caveat: Alt+F normally opens the browser menu; the page cancels that where the browser allows it, otherwise use Ctrl+Alt+F (Help says so). **Questions:** the owner wrote "the list of search items within that subject will display on the left". *Interpreted as: the Subject list sits in the narrow left column and the chosen Subject's results fill the main pane. Please confirm, or say if the results should replace the Subject list on the left instead.* Code: `diary/search.py` (`headers_only`), `diary/views.search`, `templates/diary/search.html`, `static/js/site.js`.
- **D72. Settings in the menu; Export control per device.** [Owner (round 12, item 5): "In the Menu bar, do not call it ‘Profile Settings’, but instead simply call it ‘Settings.’ Would it take up more RAM if I put a settings icon instead of the Word?" Item 6: "For ‘Export’, keep Option B when viewing on the phone, and Option E while viewing on the desktop."] **Decision (5):** the diary menu link, the page title, the heading, the saved message, Help, README and PLAN say "Settings" (the Admin's suite-level Settings on Home is unchanged; older entries above keep the old name as history). **Answer to the RAM question:** no. An inline SVG icon is a few hundred bytes of HTML per page, with no meaningful RAM or CPU difference on the server or on a phone; the only trade-off is clarity (a word is understood at once, an icon needs a tooltip). I implemented the WORD and made the swap trivial: the label is the one file `templates/diary/_settings_label.html` (the link carries `aria-label` and `title` "Settings" already). **Decision (6):** one markup, two looks chosen by a CSS media query (max-width 599px), no user-agent sniffing. Phones: Option B, an icon-only download arrow, `aria-label` and `title` "Export", at least 44px, no menu. Desktop: Option E, a split button: the main "Export" part downloads this page, the caret (`aria-haspopup="menu"`, `aria-expanded`) opens a menu **This page / Full diary / By Subject**. Keys: Down/Up on the caret open the menu, arrows/Home/End move, Esc closes and returns focus, Tab or an outside click closes. The items use the existing `export_download` endpoint with `review=1` (markers on, as the Export page ticks them by default). Readers get a plain "Export" button for This page only, no caret and no menu (the server still refuses Full for Readers). The top-bar "Export" link to the Export page stays. **Defaults:** the main part downloads at once instead of opening the Export page; on a phone Full diary and By Subject are reached through the top-bar Export link. **Questions:** is it right that the main button downloads at once rather than opening the Export page? Code: `templates/diary/_export_link.html`, `_export_label.html`, `static/css/site.css`, `static/js/site.js`. Mockups: `design/mockups/round-11/export-button/` (B and E).
- **D73. Remember me.** [Owner (round 13, item 1): "What about introducing a 'Remember Me' button at log in?"] **Decision:** the login page has a **Remember me** tick, off by default. Ticked: the session lasts 30 days (`REMEMBER_ME_DAYS`). Unticked: the session ends when the browser is closed. **Note:** before this, every login lasted the Django default of two weeks; now an unticked login is shorter. Code: `accounts/forms.py`, `accounts/views.LandingLoginView`.
- **D74. No Review tab.** [Owner (round 13, item 2): "Delete the tab 'Review' on the Header."] **Decision:** the tab is gone from the diary header. The Review page itself stays (the "for review" count on the Subjects page links to it, and Search with **For review only** lists the same records).
- **D75. Promote and demote.** [Owner (round 13, item 3): promote a Section, Sub-topic or Topic to a Sub-topic, Topic or Subject, and demote a Subject, Topic or Sub-topic to a Topic, Sub-topic or Section.] **Decision:** in Edit mode, **More** on a card offers **Promote to ...** and **Demote to ...**. A confirmation page says exactly what will happen; a demoted Subject or Topic also asks which Subject or Topic it goes under. Each change is ONE Undo step and all-or-nothing (`diary/services/levels.py`). Rules: Section to Sub-topic takes the Items after it (up to the next Section) and is made beside a Sub-topic it sat in; Sub-topic to Topic is made in the same Subject; Topic to Subject turns its Sub-topics into Topics and houses its loose Items in a Topic "1" (D61); Sub-topic to Section is added at the end of the Topic it was in, with its Items after it; Topic to Sub-topic turns its Sub-topics into Sections; Subject to Topic turns its Topics into Sub-topics (their Sub-topics become Sections) and dissolves a Topic "1". Notes move with the record, links from other Notes are re-pointed, and the old record goes to the Trash. **Limits (by design):** (the earlier limit that stopped a Sub-topic directly under an Other Subject becoming a Topic is gone, D81); a Section's For review mark is lost on promotion (a Sub-topic has none; it stays in History); a nameless Section cannot be promoted; levels below Section and above Subject do not exist. **Questions:** is it right that a demoted Topic's own Sub-topics become Sections (the only level below)? *Default: yes.*
- **D76. Search toggles.** [Owner (round 13, item 4): remove the "Search by subject" filter, add a "By Subject" toggle, and remember all Search toggles in the browser.] **Decision:** the Subject drop-down is gone from the Search page. **By Subject** is a tick beside Citations only, For review only and Search headers (it is the old By Subject view, `view=subjects`). All four ticks are remembered in this browser (`localStorage` key `quill.search.toggles`), also for the search box under the header; opening Search applies them and, if a search is showing, runs it again with them. The server still understands `subject=` in an address (the Review list's "Search within these" link uses it) but nothing on the page offers it. Code: `static/js/search-toggles.js`, `templates/diary/search.html`.
- **D77. Email plug-in.** [Owner (round 13, item 5): forgot-password with self-service reset, and scheduled emailed backups including the complete Word export.] **Decision:** see `docs/EMAIL.md`. A new `mailer` app; Django's own password-reset flow; an Admin screen at Backups > Email backups; a nightly `send_email_backups` job. Email is off until `EMAIL_HOST` is set.
- **D78. Search history and email delivery choices.** [Owner (round 13 follow-up): a search history that can be cleared; send (i) JSON only, (ii) Word only, (iii) both in one email, (iv) both in separate emails.] **Decision:** Search history is the last 50 different searches (words and ticks), kept in the browser per login (`localStorage` `quill.search.history.<user id>`), shown under the search box with click-to-repeat, remove-one and **Clear search history**. It is not stored on the server and does not follow you to another browser. Email: the schedule has one **What to send** choice with the four options; each email has its own size allowance; anything left out marks the send incomplete and raises the Admin warning bar.
- **D79. Phone: Edit button, Save, speed, Light version.** [Owner (round 13b): (1) in mobile view put the Edit button next to the Contents icon; (2) in mobile view I cannot save what I am editing; (3) it is slow on my phone, a switchable light version is fine.] **(1)** Below 900px (where the Contents icon shows) the breadcrumb row is ordered breadcrumb, Export, Edit, Contents, all together at the right. **(2) Not reproduced** (no browser or server was available): the code was hardened at the places a phone differs from a desktop. The full-screen card is `position: fixed`, which phone browsers do not shrink when the keyboard opens, so both Save buttons could be hidden; the card is now sized to `visualViewport` while open. The first field was focused at once, opening the keyboard; that no longer happens on a touch screen (`pointer: coarse`). A failed Save put its message below the bottom bar, out of sight; it now shows in a red band under the top bar and in the toast, and a 403 shows the server's own words (an expired sign-in) instead of "You cannot change this". Saving now shows "Saving..." and "Saved.". **Question for the owner:** what exactly happens when you press Save (nothing, a message, the keyboard in the way)? **(3) Speed** (estimates from reading the code, nothing was measured on a phone): (a) text pages are gzip-compressed (`config/middleware.py`, switch off with `GZIP_RESPONSES=0`; downloads are left alone); (b) a Topic or Sub-topic page loads and cleans only its own Items, Cases, Notes and links (`diary/reading.py`), where it used to load the whole Subject; (c) the scroll handler runs at most once per frame instead of on every scroll event. **Light version (WITHDRAWN by D81):** a per-user switch under My settings (off by default; `User.light_mode`, html class `light`): no animations or shadows, off-screen text is not laid out (`content-visibility`, not used while editing because it would clip menus), and Contents links open one Topic at a time whatever the profile setting says. The whole-Subject page is still reachable (Search results, Notes and the "Read Whole Subject" button need it) and is not made lighter. **Question:** which page feels slow (Subjects list, a Subject, a Topic, Search, Edit)? That decides what to do next.
- **D80. Previous / next arrows.** [Owner (round 13c): on a page showing only a Topic or Sub-topic, add left and right arrows next to the header to go to the next one; from a Sub-topic with none left in its Topic, go to the next or previous Topic.] **Decision:** the arrows sit at the right end of the Topic or Sub-topic heading (not in the sticky bar, which is crowded on a phone). Order is the Contents order (alphabetical). A Topic page steps between Topics. A Sub-topic page steps between the Sub-topics of its Topic; past either end it goes to the previous or next Topic (the one before or after its own Topic, taken literally from the instruction, so the first Sub-topic's left arrow skips its own parent Topic). A Sub-topic directly under an Other Subject only steps among its siblings. An arrow with nowhere to go is greyed. Edit mode is carried (`?edit=1`). **Questions:** should the first Sub-topic's left arrow go to its own parent Topic instead? Should the arrows also appear on the whole-Subject page? *Default: no.* Code: `diary/reading.neighbours`, `templates/diary/_step_arrows.html`, `templates/diary/reader.html`.
- **D81. Other Subjects are treated like any other Subject; the Light version is withdrawn.** [Owner (round 14): "Remove the 'light mode' concept. Further, I want Subjects in 'Other Subjects' to be treated the same as regular Subjects, that is, being able to have Topics, sub-topics and sections to organise items." Then: "What if you convert all sub-topics which do not have a parent topic into topics themselves."] **Decision, Light version:** removed (My settings switch, page styling and the Topic-at-a-time override). A migration (`accounts` 0018) drops the stored switch; backup files made while it existed still restore. The other speed changes of D79 stay. **Decision, Other Subjects:** (1) the rule "a Subject holds Topics or direct Sub-topics, not both" and the rule that kept Items directly under a Subject that held Sub-topics are gone from the service layer, so every Subject offers Item, Topic and Note, houses loose Items in Topic "1" (D61), and takes "New Topic in this Subject". (2) (REPLACED by D82 before it was used: the conversion command was withdrawn.) Following the owner's suggestion, every Sub-topic that sits directly under a Subject was to be turned into a Topic of that Subject with the same name: `python manage.py convert_direct_subtopics` (shows what would change; `--convert` does it, after a safety backup, all or nothing per profile, using the same code as **Promote**). Items, Sections and Notes keep their order and move with it, links from other Notes follow, the old Sub-topics go to the Trash (and stay in History), and loose Items are then housed in Topic "1" (`topic_one.migrate_loose`). Names show as A, B, C (Topic format) instead of 1, 2, 3 (Sub-topic format). (3) The Add menu and Move to no longer put a NEW Sub-topic straight under a Subject; the service layer still reads such data (older backup files, an unconverted database). **What still differs between Subjects on the Subjects page and Other Subjects:** they are listed in two lists (alphabetical in each); the breadcrumb of an Other Subject started with "Other Subjects" (removed by D82); the contents panel, Search "By Subject", the Export page and the Move to tree list main Subjects first and then Other Subjects; the Word export puts all Other Subjects together under one "Other Subjects" Heading 1 (each at Heading 2, their Topics at Heading 3) and "By Subject" makes one combined file for them; the Word import turns the "Miscellaneous" Topics into Other Subjects; **More > Put under Other Subjects / Show on the Subjects page** moves a Subject between the lists. Nothing else. **Questions:** should the two lists be merged into one (so there is no "Other Subjects" at all), and should the Word export give each Other Subject the same Heading 1 treatment as the others? *Default: no, not asked.*
- **D82. Direct Sub-topics go into Topic "1"; no "Other Subjects" in the breadcrumb.** [Owner (round 14b): "Remove 'other subjects' in the breadcrumb. Further, I have a thought - for any sub-topic which sits directly under a Subject, automatically create a parent Topic for it called '1'. This may already have been executed earlier. This may help prevent issues when restoring from prior backups."] **Was it done earlier?** No. Migration 0017 (D61) housed only Items and Sections, and skipped every Subject that held Sub-topics directly, so the Other Subjects' Sub-topics and their loose Items were untouched. **Decision:** this replaces the D81 conversion command (which turned each such Sub-topic into a Topic; it had not been run). (1) Data migration `diary.0018_house_direct_subtopics` moves every Sub-topic that sits directly under a Subject into that Subject's Topic "1" (made once per Subject, or the existing one reused; a trashed Subject gets a trashed Topic "1"), keeping the Sub-topics' ids, order, Items, Sections and Notes, so links and addresses keep working; then it houses the Items and Sections that 0017 had left loose under such Subjects in the same Topic "1". Count checks and "migrate" history rows as in 0017; any mismatch rolls the whole migration back; the deploy's pre-migration backup is taken first (`migrate_with_backup`). (2) **Old backups:** the restore loader runs the same mapping on the file (`subtopic_one.house_document`, then `topic_one.house_document`), and the restore preview and the log say how many were mapped, so a backup made before this restores in the new shape. (3) **New records:** a Sub-topic added, moved or restored under a Subject goes into its Topic "1", like Items and Sections (`housing.HOUSED`). (4) `manage.py house_loose_items` now also houses Sub-topics; run it after a fresh Word import, which still places the "Miscellaneous" Sub-topics directly under their Subjects so its reconcile can compare with the file. (5) The breadcrumb no longer has the "Other Subjects" step for any Subject. **Effect on screen:** an Other Subject now shows one Topic "1" (numbered with the Topic format, for example "A. 1") holding its former Sub-topics, which keep their Sub-topic numbers (1, 2, 3). **Question:** keep the name "1", or would you prefer a word such as "General"? *Default: "1", as asked.* Code: `diary/subtopic_one.py`, `diary/migrations/0018_house_direct_subtopics.py`, `diary/services/housing.py`, `backups/services.py`.
- **D83. Search: wider page; Group by Subject is a display switch.** [Owner (round 15): "(i) the search screen to be wider ... when I am in 'By Subject' mode, it is a bit cramped. Further, 'By Subject' should not be a search feature, but a grouping feature - so, I should be able to toggle it on and off without re-doing the search."] **Decision:** the Search page is up to 72rem wide (it was 46rem, the reading width); the search box and ticks stay at 46rem so they do not stretch; the Subject column of the grouped view is up to 19rem. "By Subject" is no longer a tick among the search filters: a **Group by Subject** switch (`role="switch"`) sits beside the result count, only when results are shown and no single Subject is being searched. Flipping it, and picking a Subject in the grouped view, swap the results in place (`static/js/search-toggles.js` fetches the same address in the other layout and replaces only the results; the address is updated with `replaceState`), so the words, ticks and scroll position are untouched and Search is not pressed again. The server does run the query again for the new layout (about a tenth of a second at today's size); **alternative not chosen:** grouping the already-shown results in the browser, which would only group the first 50 and break "Show more". The choice is remembered by the browser on its own (`quill.search.group`), applies to the header search box too, and is no longer stored with Search history entries (a history entry is a search; grouping is a view). Without JavaScript the switch is an ordinary link. Supersedes the "By Subject" tick of D76.
- **D84. Header bar: Icons or Words.** [Owner (round 15): "In settings, give me an option to have either icons or words displayed in the header bar. If icons, then simple icons will be displayed for each of the header bar items (Trash will be a dustbin, Export a downwards arrow, Help a question mark, Settings a gear, and Search and Home the existing Icons). If Words, then display the words instead."] **Decision:** My settings has **Header bar: Icons / Words**, per user (`User.header_style`, so no extra query per page; migration `accounts` 0019; older backups restore with the default). **Icons:** Trash is a dustbin, Export a downward arrow, Help a question mark in a circle, Settings a gear, Search the magnifier and Home the house; each keeps its name as `aria-label` (screen readers) and `title` (tooltip), at least 44px to tap. **Words:** Trash, Export, Help, Settings, Search and Home as words. The current page stays underlined in both. Back and Forward stay arrows in both (not listed by the owner). It applies to the diary header bar; the Home button follows it everywhere it appears; the suite Home page's own links (Profiles, Users, Import, Backups, Settings, Help, My settings) are unchanged. **Default (pending owner confirmation): Icons**, closest to the owner's last header request (D71: Search as an icon only). Icons are inline SVG in `templates/icons/` (a few hundred bytes each; no image files, no extra requests). Supersedes the Settings word of D72.
- **D85. No boxes around icons; icons on the main Home page.** [Owner (round 15): "Throughout the program, remove the boxes around the icons. Design Icons for the main home page as well."] **Decision:** every icon-only control loses its border and background: the Home and Search icons, Back and Forward, the header menu (three lines), the Contents icon, the Contents panel's Expand all and Collapse all, the previous/next arrows and, on a phone, the Export arrow. They keep a 44px tap area and the keyboard focus ring; hover turns them oxblood; an open Contents panel turns its icon oxblood instead of boxing it. Buttons that carry a word (Save, Cancel, + Add, Edit, Export with its caret on a computer, the Edit switch) keep their look. In Words mode, Search, Home and Log out read as plain header words. **Main Home page icons** (same Icons / Words choice, D84): Profiles two stacked notebooks, Users two people, Import a document with an arrow going in, Backups an archive box, Settings the gear, Help the question mark, My settings three sliders, Log out a door with an arrow going out; each has its name as tooltip and for screen readers; Admin-only links stay Admin-only. Code: `templates/icons/`, `templates/_nav_item.html`, `accounts/context_processors.suite_nav`, `static/css/site.css`. **Question:** are these pictures clear enough, or would you like any of them changed?
- **D86. Home page: three merged pages with tabs.** [Owner (round 15): "In Home: merge Users & Profiles into a single page, with separate sub-pages. Merge Backups & Import into a single page, with separate sub-pages. Merge both Settings pages into a single page, with separate sub-pages."] **Decision:** the Home header has four items: **Users & Profiles** (tabs Users, Profiles), **Backups & Import** (tabs Backups, Email backups, Import), **Settings** (tabs My settings, for everyone, and Site settings, for the Admin: the display names of Home and the diary) and **Help**. Each item opens its first tab and stays marked on every page of the group (including the add, edit, delete and restore pages under it); the tabs sit under the header, the current one underlined. A reader sees Settings (no tab row, since only My settings is theirs) and Help. Every address is unchanged, so bookmarks and links still work; each tab is still its own page (only one is loaded at a time). Icons: Users & Profiles the two people, Backups & Import the archive box, Settings the gear, Help the question mark; the Profiles, Import and My settings icons of D85 are no longer used in the header. The diary's own Settings page (export name, display name, Contents links) is unchanged: it belongs to the diary, not to Home. **Question:** should the diary Settings page also become a tab of Home's Settings? *Default: no; it is per profile and for Editors.* Code: `accounts/context_processors.SUITE_GROUPS`, `templates/base_suite_home.html`, `static/css/site.css`.
- **D87. Keep my place; hyperlinks on citations with an on/off switch.** [Owner (round 16, items 1 and 2): "When the page refreshes for whatever reason, I lose my spot as it goes up to the top." "I want to be able to add Hyperlinks to Citations. However, I also want to be able to turn off the feature ... perhaps keep it as a toggle in the header bar, but only if you are able to fix the problem where, if the page refreshes when I click this, it would reload to the top ... The hyperlink address must be maintained EXACTLY as entered."] **Why it went to the top:** after many edits the diary reloads the page, and a script then jumped to the entry named in the address (for example the Search result you came from) instead of where you were; forms that send you back (Undo, Move, Delete, Done) and switching Edit on or off are new page loads, which start at the top. **Decision (place):** `static/js/keep-place.js` remembers, per page and per browser tab, the entry at the top of the screen and how far down it sat (and the plain scroll height on pages without entries), and restores it after a reload (yours, the phone's, or the diary's own), after the browser's or the diary's Back/Forward, after a form that returns to the same page, and when Edit is switched on or off. A fresh visit through a link still opens at the top or at the entry it points to. **Decision (links):** each citation (Case) has an optional **Link** (`Case.url`, migration `diary` 0019; older backups restore with no links). It is stored exactly as typed (capitals, query and fragment untouched); only spaces before and after are dropped. It must start with http:// or https:// (so a link can never run a script), be one line with no spaces, at most 2000 characters; otherwise the save is refused with the reason and what was typed is kept on screen. It is entered in the Item editor, the editing card and the Add form, kept in History and Undo.
