In the reading view a linked citation is underlined and opens in a new tab. **Switch:** a chain-link icon (or the word "Links") in the diary header bar turns the links off and on for this browser; it only changes how the page looks, so nothing reloads and the place cannot be lost; it is applied before the page is drawn, so there is no flicker. Default: on. **Not done (ask if wanted):** links in the Word export; showing the address in Search. Code: `diary/services/records.clean_url`, `templates/diary/_item.html`, `static/js/keep-place.js`, `static/js/site.js`.
- **D88. For review is merged into the Flags; Pending; a drop-down; Sections flagged.** [Owner (round 16, item 3): "Merge 'For Review' into 'Flags'. Put the text of every item labelled 'For Review' into a Flag as 'Other'. Add one more flag, after 'None', called 'Pending'. Make the flags a drop down menu. Allow me to add Text beside all flags, EXCEPT none. Delete the 'For Review' text on the 'Subjects' page." The owner then accepted the recommendations.] **Decision:** flags are None, Pending, Overruled, Doubted, Other, chosen from a drop-down in the Item editor, the editing cards and both Add forms; every flag but None takes text (the box is greyed out while None is chosen; text beside None is never kept); flag text is one line of up to 2000 characters. **Sections take a flag too.** On screen and in Word a flag reads "[Pending: text]" (Other shows its text alone; text that already starts with the flag's name is shown alone, so "[Overruled by X]" stays as it was). **The data** (migrations `diary` 0020 schema, 0021 data, 0022 check rule; shared rules in `diary/review_flags.py`): an Item or Section marked For review with no flag becomes Other with the review note as its text, or "For review" when it had no note; one that already had a flag keeps it and gets " · For review: <note>" added to its text. A note left on a record that was NOT marked (an old switch turned off) is not a flag and is left untouched in the retired column. The For review columns stay as LEGACY columns, kept off by a check rule (the same pattern as the old Item citation column), so old backups and history still read. **Older backups** are mapped the same way before they are restored (the restore preview and log say how many); **History restore** of a version saved before the merge folds its mark into the flag the same way. **The Word import** writes its own notes ("Imported whole as Text ...", the Section note) as the flag Other. **Removed:** the For review switch and note everywhere, the one-click For review endpoint, the "for review" count on the Subjects page, Search's "For review only" (now **Flags**, D90), the Review list page (its address forwards to the Flags page, D89). **Word export:** "Include review markers" became **Include flags** and now covers every flag (it is still ticked by default; the round-trip check leaves flags out, as before). **Questions:** none outstanding.
- **D89. The Flags tab: Flags and Hyperlinks.** [Owner (round 16, item 5): a new top-bar tab "Flags" (a flag symbol) with two sub-tabs. Flags: view all items with flags, or one type; search only within the text beside a flag; display like Search grouped by Subject. Hyperlinks: view all citations with links, or without; search within the hyperlink text; display the same way.] **Decision:** the diary header bar has **Flags** (a flag icon, or the word) for everyone with a profile; its pages have the tabs Flags and Hyperlinks under the title. **Flags** lists every flagged Item and Section of the profile (Trash excluded), with **Show: All flags / Pending / Overruled / Doubted / Other** and a search box that looks only in the flag text; **Hyperlinks** lists each citation that has a link (the search box looks in the address) or, with **Without a link**, each one that has none (the search box looks in the citation). Both use the exact grouped layout of Search (one shared template and helper: Subjects with counts on the left, the chosen Subject's results on the right, the first Subject when none is chosen, a back link on phones, "Show more" past 50, picking a Subject swaps in place). Every result opens the record in context. The old Review address forwards here. Code: `diary/views.flags_page`, `flag_links`, `_grouped`; `diary/search.run_search(flags_only, flag_kind)`, `run_links`; `templates/diary/flags.html`, `flag_links.html`, `_grouped_results.html`, `_result_card.html`.
- **D90. Search ticks: Group by Subject, Citations, Flags, Headers; History on the same line.** [Owner (round 16, item 4).] **Decision:** one line under the search box: **History** (a small drop-down of recent searches, with Clear), then **Group by Subject** (still a display switch, D83: flipping it never runs the search again), **Citations** (was "Citations only"), **Flags** (new: looks only in the text beside flags; with no words it lists every flagged record; an older address or remembered choice saying For review only means Flags), **Headers** (was "Search headers"). The meanings of Citations and Headers are unchanged. Ticks, grouping and history are still remembered by the browser.
- **Round 16: hints in empty boxes.** [Owner: "when I add an item / topic etc, the text boxes contain greyed out text inside (when empty) ... However, when editing, that greyed out text is absent."] **Decision:** the rich text boxes (Item text, Note text, citation comments) now show their grey hint whenever they are empty, like the plain boxes; and every editing box has one: "Text of the Item", "Case name [(2020) 1 SCC 1]", "Comment (optional)", "Link (optional): https://...", "Name", "Text beside the flag (optional)".
- **D91. Forgot my password by username, with plain answers.** [Owner (round 17): "let the User type in their user name, rather than email address." Then: "If the username exists, say 'Email sent'. If the username exists but there is no email address attached, say 'No recovery email address found with this user.' If the username does not exist, say 'User not found.'"] **Decision:** the page asks for the username (capitals and spaces around it ignored); the link goes to the email address on that account and works for one hour. The answers are exactly the owner's three; a disabled account counts as "User not found". **Trade-off accepted by the owner:** the page now says whether a username exists. The limits stay (3 requests an hour per account, 10 per visitor); over them the page says "Too many requests. Please try again in an hour." A failure to send says so. Code: `accounts/forms.UsernamePasswordResetForm`, `accounts/views.ForgotPasswordView`.
- **D92. My edits.** [Owner (round 17): "I want to be able to see my Edit history."] **Decision:** Settings > **My edits** lists the signed-in user's own changes in their profile, newest first, one line per action (an action that changed several records, such as deleting a Topic with its Items, is one line with "and N more"): when (IST), what was done and to what kind of record, its name or first words, **Open** (the record in context, unless it is in the Trash) and **History** (Editors). 100 actions at a time, "Show more" up to 1,000. It shows what the edit history keeps (30 days; the newest version of every record is always kept). Code: `diary/views_history.my_edits`, `templates/diary/my_edits.html`.
- **D93. One Settings area with tabs; Change my password.** [Owner (round 17): "re-organise the various settings options (in sub-tabs, if necessary) to make them more readable / accessible."] **Decision:** the gear (Home and diary header bars) opens **Settings**, with tabs under the header: **Account** (email address; Change my password), **Display** (header bar icons or words; what opens after login), **Numbers** (number formats with the preview), **Word export** (date in file names), **Diary** (the profile's Display Name, export name and Contents links; Editors change them, everyone sees them), **My edits** (D92) and, for the Admin, **Site** (the Home and diary names). Without a diary profile only Account and Display show. The first four are one page shown a tab at a time: every field stays in the form and the other tabs are only hidden, so Save never changes a tab you did not open, and a tab with an error opens by itself; Save returns to the same tab. Every older address still works (`/my-settings/`, `/caselaw/profile-settings/`, `/settings/`). **Change my password** (current password, then the new one twice, the usual rules) keeps you signed in. Code: `accounts/context_processors.SUITE_GROUPS`, `templates/accounts/my_settings.html`, `templates/accounts/password_change.html`.
- **D94. Deleted users can be restored for 30 days.** [Owner (round 18): "Can you allow me to restore a deleted User, for upto 30 days?" Then: "Yes" to keeping the username reserved.] **Decision:** Delete on the Users tab no longer removes the account. It is switched off (no sign-in; any open session stops) and listed under **Deleted users** with who deleted it, when, and the days left, and a **Restore** button that brings it back exactly as it was (profiles, roles, settings, and whether it could sign in). Nothing else is touched while it is deleted; its username and email address stay taken. After `USER_RETENTION_DAYS` (default 30) the nightly job (`purge_deleted_profiles`, which now does users first, then profiles) removes it for good, only if that night's backup succeeded, and writes a purge log; its past edits stay in the history without a name. A deleted user cannot be edited (restore first) and does not count as a profile member. New columns `User.deleted_at`, `deleted_by_name` (a name, not a link, so backup files never depend on load order) and `was_active` (accounts migration 0020). Code: `accounts/user_trash.py`.
- **D95. A user may have several profiles.** [Owner (round 18): "Will it be possible to enable a User to have multiple profiles activated, per program - if so, do it?"] **Decision:** the rule "one profile per user per program" is replaced by "one membership per user and profile" (accounts migration 0020), and each membership keeps its own role (Editor in one profile, Reader in another). The user form shows one **No access / Editor / Reader** choice per live profile; the Users list shows "Main (Editor), Junior (Reader)". The profile in use is the one switched to most recently (`ProfileMembership.last_used_at`, so it follows the person to every device); before any switch, the first by name. Someone with several profiles switches from the small arrow next to the name at the top of the diary or from the buttons on the Home page; switching opens that profile's Subjects page, and every page, permission, Undo and setting then follows that profile. Restoring a deleted profile is no longer blocked by its members having other profiles. Number formats, the opening page and the header choice stay per person (not per profile); an opening Subject or last page is only used in the profile it belongs to (as before). Code: `accounts/profiles.live_memberships`, `use_profile`, `set_user_profiles`; `accounts/views.profile_switch`; `templates/diary/_profile_switch.html`.
- **D96. Settings regrouped: Admins, General, the diary, Help.** [Owner (round 18): "(1) Admins: subtabs will be Users, Profiles, Page Names (what is now 'Site'), Backups, Email Backups (remove the back button to Backups from this tab), Import, Export and Edit History [Admin only] (2) General: Account, Display [Merge 'Diary' into Display.] (3) Case Law Diary [named after Page Names]: Numbers & My Edits." Edit History and Export were then dropped from Admins; the diary settings stay Editor-only inside Display.] **Decision:** the Home header has **Admins** (Admin only: Users, Profiles, Page Names, Backups, Email Backups, Import), **General** (Account, Display), an item named after the diary (Page Names > Case law diary name; tabs Numbers and My edits; only for someone with a diary profile) and **Help**. **Page Names** is the old Site page, now saying what each box changes. **Display** holds the old Diary tab's Display Name and Contents settings (shared by the profile: everyone sees them, Editors change them; they are only saved when the page sent them, so another save never resets them). The diary gear opens General. The old addresses still work (`/caselaw/profile-settings/` opens Display). Code: `accounts/context_processors.SUITE_GROUPS`.
- **D97. One Export control; the file name and date on the Export page.** [Owner (round 18): "Remove the 'Export' tab which shows up on each page, even as you scroll down. Export will now only be a Tab on the Header row." And: "The selector in 'Word Export' and the text box for 'Export File Name' ... should both be moved to the 'Export to Word' page. ... Let Export File Name be changeable by 'Readers' too."] **Decision:** the Export split button in the sticky breadcrumb row is gone (with its This page / Full diary / By Subject menu); the header's **Export** is the only control, and on a Subject, Topic or Sub-topic page it opens the Export page ready to download that page. The Export page has a **File name and date** section: the export file name (the profile's, shown with the "Files will start with" preview; anyone using the profile may change it) and the date format (each person's own). Code: `diary/views_export.export_settings`, `diary/forms.ExportSettingsForm`.
- **D98. Offline copy; every member may export everything.** [Owner (round 19): "Build an offline copy. Let me download it through the Export tab. I do not want to edit offline. No, I do not want it to arrive with the scheduled email backups." And: "Let all users who have access to the profile (be they Admins, Editors or Readers) be able to export the entire Word file or Offline copy, or any parts thereof, in the same manner."] **Decision (file):** the **Offline copy** is ONE self-contained `.html` file (its style sheet and script are inside it; no request to any server). It looks like the reading pages: a Subjects page (Subjects with Item counts, then Other Subjects), each Subject with its Contents, Topics, Sub-topics, Sections, Items numbered by the downloader's Numbers settings, citations (a citation with a web address is a link opening in a new tab; only http:// and https:// addresses become links), flags (when "Include flags" is ticked), Notes and "See also" links that jump to the target inside the file (a target in Trash reads "target deleted"). A search box works in the browser (every word typed must appear; up to 300 results; each result jumps to its place). It is read-only: no forms, no edit controls, nothing is sent anywhere. It holds only the profile's live data (no Trash), read with the same `gather.load` as the Word export, and is built in `diary/wordexport/offline.py` (assets in `offline_assets/`). It is refreshed by downloading it again. It works best on a computer; on a phone the Word file is easier. File name: `[export name] Offline copy [date].html` (the same name and date settings as Word). **Not in the email backups:** `mailer` and `send_email_backups` are unchanged. **Decision (who):** this replaces D48. Everyone who uses the profile (Admin, Editor or Reader) can download every export: Full, Full by Subject, a Subject, the Current View, and the Offline copy of the whole diary, a Subject, or a Topic or Sub-topic. Someone without the profile still gets 403/404 and another profile's records give "not found". **Size handling:** like the Word Full export, it is built in one request from about ten queries and sent as one response (about a few MB for thousands of Items, text only, no images); no paging was added. If a very large diary ever approaches the 60 s limit, the fix would be a background job. Code: `diary/views_export.export_download` (types `offline`, `offline_subject`, `offline_view`), `service.offline_copy`.
- **D99. Header: Offline (arrow) and Export (page with a W).** [Owner (round 20): "In the header row, keep a downward arrow icon. This will automatically download the Offline Copy." And: "Make them 'Export' and 'Offline', when in Words. In icons, go with 'B'. I agree to both of points 1 & 2."] **Decision:** the diary header bar has a new item **Offline**, right after Export: a direct download (no page in between) of the Offline copy of the WHOLE diary of the profile in use, flags included (`export_download?type=offline&review=1`). It keeps the downward arrow (`icons/offline.svg.html`, the old Export icon); in Words it reads "Offline"; `aria-label` "Offline", `title` "Offline copy: download the whole diary to read without internet". **Export** keeps opening the Export page (with the round 18 scope on a Subject, Topic or Sub-topic) and now has a new icon, a page with a W (`icons/word.svg.html`); its word stays "Export". Both are shown to everyone with the profile (Admin, Editor, Reader; D98). Supersedes the icon of D84 (Export was a downward arrow).
- **D100. Secret Offline link (no sign-in, no password).** [Owner (round 21): "What about a dedicated page, through which I can download the offline link, even if I am not a user? Will that cause any other issues" After the risks and a safer design were explained: "I like the secret link concept. The purpose of this is to be able to download the entire diary for offline viewing on my phone, in places with poor network. I fear that having a password will only complicate matters, by adding an extra layer of delay."] **Decision:** each profile can have ONE secret link, made, switched on or off, and renewed by an Admin on **Admins > Profiles > Offline link**. Anyone who has the link can download the saved Offline copy of that profile without signing in. There is **no password** (the owner's choice, to avoid delay on a poor connection). **Token:** `secrets.token_urlsafe(32)`, stored in plain text in the database (`accounts.OfflineLink`) so the Admin can copy the link again. Trade-off: anyone with access to the database could read it. It is looked up by its sha256 hash and compared in constant time. It appears in the web server's request log because it is part of the address; the app's own log lines leave it out (the explicit log calls omit it and a filter on the console handler, `diary.logfilters.RedactOfflineToken`, redacts it from Django's request warnings for 4xx responses), but the web server's access log still shows it, so anyone with log access can see a live token. **Responses:** the pages carry `X-Robots-Tag: noindex, nofollow, noarchive`, `Referrer-Policy: no-referrer` and `Cache-Control: private, no-store` (`no-cache` for the file). Every wrong, switched-off or deleted link gives the same small 404 page, so nothing is revealed. **The saved copy:** built only by the Admin's **Make a copy now** button or by the nightly job `refresh_offline_copies` (run after the backup, in `render.yaml`; a failure there never stops the backup, the purge or the emails, and the last error is shown to the Admin as `copy_error`). So the copy may be **up to a day old**. A visitor never makes the file, which keeps the open page light. The copy is stored in the database (no disk) as is (not gzipped), holds live data only (no Trash), includes flags, and uses the number and date formats of the Admin who last made it (standard formats if there is none). **Public page:** tiny, with a big **Download** button and the date, size and number of Items of the copy. The download supports `Range` so an interrupted download on a weak signal can resume. **Limits:** 20 downloads per hour per visitor and 100 per hour per link (a 429 page asks to try again later); a retry from byte 0 counts as a new download. A **resumed range** (`Range` starting above 0) is counted against a looser separate cap (60 per hour per visitor, 300 per link) so a weak signal can resume often but the header cannot bypass the limit; HEAD is answered from stored metadata (no blob read) and is not counted. The visitor address is the LAST `X-Forwarded-For` entry (added by Render's proxy; the first is client-supplied and never trusted). Ranges are sliced from the stored bytes without extra copies, and the nightly refresh defers the old blob so memory stays low on the 512 MB instance. **Backups:** the links and copies are NOT in backup files (derived data, and the token is a secret); a restore removes all links, so the Admin makes a new one. **Other rules:** Delete all data erases the saved copy; deleting a profile (soft delete) makes its link stop working. **No expiry** (the owner did not ask for one). **Phone note:** on an iPhone, Files or Quick Look may not run the page's JavaScript, so the search box may not work; open the file in Safari or Chrome. Android Chrome works. **Risk accepted:** a leaked link shows the whole diary until an Admin switches it off or makes a new link; the owner chose convenience over a password. Code: `diary/views_offline_link.py`, `diary/offline_link.py`, `accounts.views.offline_link_admin`, `templates/accounts/offline_link.html`, `accounts/management/commands/refresh_offline_copies.py`.
- **D101. Offline goes back into Export; Export has the downward arrow again.** [Owner (round 22): "Merge the offline download tab in the Header back into Export, and once again give Export the downward arrow icon."] **Decision:** this reverses the header item of D99. The header has one **Export** item again (opens the Export page, with the round 18 scope on a Subject, Topic or Sub-topic) and it uses the downward arrow (`icons/export.svg.html`, as in D84). The separate **Offline** header item and the page-with-a-W icon are gone. The Offline copy is still downloaded from the Export page (D98, open to Admins, Editors and Readers), `export_download?type=offline` is unchanged, and the secret link of D100 is untouched.

## Round diary-round

- **Item 1.8. Search operators.** [Owner: "In search, incorporate boolean features, search as NOT (which display search results which do not have a particular search term) - what else can you suggest?" Follow-up: "happy with all choices; go with defaults."] **Decision (the suggested set, all approved):**
  - **Operators.** AND is the default between words (`AND` may also be written). `OR` (`fraud OR forgery`). `NOT criminal` or `-criminal` (also `-(a OR b)` and `-"a phrase"`). Exact phrase in double quotes (as before). Brackets group (`(fraud OR forgery) -criminal`); AND binds tighter than OR. Prefix wildcard `arbitra*`: the word must START with those letters (a plain word still matches inside a word, as before). Citation search is the existing word-and-number matching inside citations (`SCC 2016` finds `(2016) 4 SCC 12`); no new syntax. `subject:Arbitration fraud` keeps only records in Subjects whose name contains the piece (`subject:"Civil Procedure"` for several words; several `subject:` pieces mean any of them). `flag:pending`, `flag:review`, `flag:other` keep only records with that flag; `review` means Overruled or Doubted (the flags that ask for a second look), and `flag:overruled` and `flag:doubted` also work. `flag:` alone lists every record with that flag; it combines with the Flags tab's own kind choice (both must hold).
  - **Capitals.** `AND`, `OR`, `NOT` are operators only in capitals, so an ordinary lower-case "or" or "not" in a search stays a word. A leading `-` is NOT only at the start of a word (`SCC-49` is still `SCC 49`).
  - **Brackets that used to be ignored.** `(2018) 9 SCC 49` still finds the citation: it reads as the word 2018 AND 9 AND SCC AND 49.
  - **Malformed queries never error.** Unbalanced brackets, `()`, `OR`/`AND`/`NOT` with nothing to work on, `OR` beside a `subject:`/`flag:` filter, a negated filter (`-flag:x`), an unknown `flag:` kind, an empty `subject:`, or brackets nested more than 20 deep: the query is searched as plain words (operators and brackets dropped, quoted phrases kept) and the results show one short grey line saying so ("The operators in this search could not be read ... See Search tips."). An unterminated quote still makes a phrase to the end, as before, with no notice.
  - **NOT semantics.** A record matches `-term` when none of its searched fields holds the term; a query of only NOT words lists every other record (in the Citations / Flags / Headers modes, of those records).
  - **Highlighting and ranking.** Only positive terms (not under a NOT) are highlighted; ranking still puts records holding all the positive words together first.
  - **Where.** Search, the Flags page and the Hyperlinks page all use the operators (`searchtext.parse_search`); the Offline copy's own search box is unchanged. The old `parse_query` stays for callers that want plain terms.
  - **Search tips.** A "Search tips" panel (a closed `<details>`, id `search-tips`) under the search box on the Search page lists each operator with an example; Help describes them.
  - **Speed.** Still one pass in Python over the same records, with the same number of database queries (a test compares them). Each term is looked for once per record whatever the operators; `test_searchtext.py` keeps the 5,000-record measurement and adds an operator query over 5,000 records (asserts under 1.0 s).

- **Items 1.1 to 1.7. Diary behaviour round.** [Owner: "1.1. When demoting a Topic to a Sub-Topic, etc, the current list is too long - I do not want to see every single topic from every subject all at once. Instead, use a similar system as is used for moving things. Replicate that system here. However, I want to make one change - when moving / demoting, the 'Current' subject / topic / etc should be highlighted in another colour, not red. Perhaps blue?" "1.2. Bug, where 'Save' or 'Cancel' sometimes does not close the window. When adding a new judgment, 'Save' should save and close, not save and open a new judgment to add." "1.3. In 'Desktop' view, while adding / editing citations, let the text box expand to incorporate additional text (i.e. text wrapping) - but I DO NOT want multi-line output." "1.4. In multi-citation items, when citations are to be shown on separate lines, add a \":\" after the main 'Text'." "1.5. Give me an option to JUSTIFY all text which is ordinarily left-aligned." "1.6. In flags, both 'Other' and 'Pending' should share the same display method (Yellow box)." "1.7. Let 'Notes' be placed exactly below the Topic / Sub-Topic header, and a bit indented, so that it is clear to what it is assigned." Follow-up: "happy with all choices; go with defaults. Do not restyle anything; behaviour only."] **Decisions (the defaults):**
  - **1.1 Demote uses the Move to picker.** `edit_level.html` no longer has the long drop-down of every Topic of every Subject. Demoting a Topic shows the Subjects as headings with their Topics under them (the arrows, Expand all and Collapse all are the same as Move to; the markup is shared: `_picker_box.html` and `_picker_nodes.html`, with `by_id` making each choice post `target=<id>`). Demoting a Subject lists the other Subjects. Choosing a place shows the usual "what changes" page with the place named and a Demote button (and "Choose another ..."); the service rules and the confirm step are unchanged (`views_edit.level_entries`). The place the record is in now is drawn in blue (`.picker-current`, `#1f5fbf`, the existing focus blue) in both Move to and Demote; red stays for alerts and danger. Move to still marks the Subject the record is in (its text "current" is kept); Demote marks the Subject the Topic is in.
  - **1.2 Save and Cancel.** Root cause: the Add form (`_inline_add.html`) had a Cancel button (`data-inline-cancel`) but `edit-mode.js` only bound that button for the editing card, so Cancel on the Add form did nothing (only Esc closed it); and after saving an Item the script deliberately opened a new blank Add form under it, which looked like the window not closing. Cancel is now handled in `setupAdd` (asking first if something was typed, as for the card) and Save, or Enter, saves and closes. "Save and add another" is gone (there was never a separate button for it); the hint under the form says "Enter saves and closes". The Edit card was already correct. A test (`test_diary_round.py`) reads the script to keep both.
  - **1.3 Citation box.** On the Add form, the editing card and the Item editor page the citation is a one-row `<textarea class="single-line" data-single-line>` (same look as the text box) that `static/js/single-line.js` grows to its text on desktop (width at least 600px). The value is always one line: Enter does not add a break (it saves the form like Enter in a one-line box), pasted or dropped line breaks become spaces, and the server still joins any line breaks into spaces. The Item text box is the rich text box, which already wraps and grows. Without the script the box is a one-row box that still works.
  - **1.4 Colon.** With several citations on separate lines the Item text ends with ":" (a `<span class="item-colon">` on screen, the Offline copy and a plain ":" in the Word export), unless the text already ends with ":" (never doubled) or there is no text. Inline layout and single citations are unchanged. The Word round-trip check compares against an imported file, which has no Items with their citations on separate lines, so it is unaffected; its list of deliberate differences says so. An Item that is converted from a nested list and exported will therefore read "Text:" followed by its (a), (b) lines.
  - **1.5 Justify text.** My settings > Display > **Justify text** (a tick box, off by default) is stored per user as `accounts.User.justify_text` (migration `accounts/0022_user_justify_text`, like `header_style`). `accounts.context_processors.header_style` also gives `justify_text` (no extra query) and `base_suite.html` puts `class="justify-text"` on `<html>`. The CSS (`@media screen` only, so print and the Word export are unaffected) justifies the Item text, Note text, Section names and the citation lines/comments. A post of My settings without the tick box turns it off, as any tick box does.
  - **1.6 Flags.** Other and Pending both use the yellow (amber) box (`.flag-text.flag-pending, .flag-text.flag-other`, also in the Offline copy). Overruled and Doubted keep the flag colour. The Word export still writes every flag in red text, as before.
  - **1.7 Notes under the heading.** A Topic's or Sub-topic's own Notes are listed straight after its heading (before its Items and Sections) and indented 1.25rem (`.note.heading-note`), in reading and Edit mode alike. A Subject's Notes, and the Notes of a Section or an Item, stay where they were. The Word export is unchanged (it still prints a heading's Notes after its Items).

- **Item 3.1. Words or Logo for everyone.** [Owner: "3.Home: 3.1. In Settings / Display, keep separate options for Words / Logos for each of Home, Case Law Diary and Fee Register."] [Answer: "I am happy with all of your choices, except for 3.1 - let those options be for everyone; if it has to be moved to a different page if not currently visible to all then do that."] **Decision:** the header bar choice (D84: Logo = icons, Words = the names) is no longer a personal setting on Settings > Display (the option and its use of `accounts.User.header_style` are gone; the column stays in the table, unused, so no data is lost and older backups still load). It is one suite-wide choice for each of Home, the Case Law Diary and the Fee Register, on **Admins > Page Names** (Admin only; the other users cannot see Admins), next to the names. The Fee Register choice is shown only while the Fee Register is switched on (`FEES_ENABLED`). Default: Logo, as before. Storage: `SuiteName` rows keyed `brand_home`, `brand_diary`, `brand_fees` whose `display_name` is `words` or `logo` (`accounts.suite.get_brand_modes` / `set_brand_modes`; no migration; `get_names` ignores them; backups already include `SuiteName`). One query per page serves both the names and the choice (`suite.read_all`). Template variables: `suite_brand` (`{"home"|"diary"|"fees": "words"|"logo"}`), `home_header_words` (True when Home shows words) and `header_words` (as before, now the choice of the page's program: the Fee Register's pages use the Fee Register choice, every other page the Case Law Diary choice). `base_suite_home.html` (the Home, Admins, Settings and Help pages) uses `home_header_words` for its header, so Home has its own choice. A post of Page Names without the choice keeps the current one.
- **Item 3.2. An Admin is an Admin everywhere.** [Answer: "Also, as a global rule, an 'Admin' is an 'Admin' for all sub-programs."] **Decision:** a suite Admin (`user.is_admin`) is an Editor in every program and every live profile, with or without a membership, and is never shown or treated as a Reader. They can open and switch to every live profile of every program (the header switcher and the Home page list them all; the Profiles screen already listed all). Implemented in `accounts.profiles.live_memberships` (so `program_role`, `program_editor_required`, `program_reader_required`, `request_role`, `request_profile` and the Fee Register decorators inherit it unchanged): the stored memberships are returned with the role Editor (in memory only; the stored role is untouched and the Users screens still show it, via `raw=True`), followed by an unsaved stand-in Editor membership for each live profile the Admin has none in. A non-Admin still costs one query; an Admin costs one more (the profiles list). **Current profile of an Admin:** their own most recently used profile; else their own first by name; if they have no membership in the program, the first live profile of the program by name, until they switch (switching records a Reader membership to remember the choice; it gives no extra power and the Admin still works as an Editor). Tests whose expectation changed because of this rule: `accounts.tests.test_accounts.test_decorators` (an Admin with a Reader membership is now passed by the Editor decorator), `diary.tests.test_views_trash_history` (an Admin who is only a Reader of the profile may now delete permanently, was 403) and `diary.tests.test_views.test_home_shows_program_to_admin_without_membership` (an Admin without a membership now sees the program and opens it, was "No programs are available" and 403).


## Round redesign: Home and the Case Law Diary in the desktop Fee Register language

Owner instruction, verbatim: "I like the fee register desktop app's design a lot, and would like that copied wholesale into the website" and, after seeing the mockups, "I like all of them except the 'Subjects' page. I like the current format over the table format in the screenshot. So stick to the current format there."

- Home, Settings, Admins, Help, login and password pages, the error pages and every Case Law Diary page now use the same look as the Fee Register: warm paper, black ink, one maroon accent (#7A2E2E), italic serif program name, plain-text nav with a maroon underline on the current item, underlined search field, serif titles, small uppercase labels, hairline rules, flat 2px corners.
- Shared files: `static/css/tokens.css` (palette, fonts), `static/css/design.css` (top bar and tabs, for `body.ledger` and `body.look`), `static/css/look.css` (Home and Diary page layer, `body.look` only). The Fee Register still adds `ledger.css` and `fees.css`.
- The Subjects page (`templates/diary/subjects.html`) keeps its list format, elements, order and behaviour. It only takes the new colours, fonts and header. It is not a table.
- Owner settings are unchanged: Words or Logo per program, Links toggle, justify text, view whole subject, Icons or Words header.
- Test changes made for the redesign: the edit-mode body class is now `look edit-on` (`diary/tests/test_views_edit.py`).
- The offline copy (`diary/wordexport/offline_assets/offline.css`) was recoloured to the same palette and fonts; its structure is unchanged. The Word export is untouched.


## Round: financial year drop-downs, items per page, Edit Statistics in Settings, Log out icon

Owner instructions, verbatim: "When selecting the financial year, there should be a drop down menu, not a place to type. Give me an option in settings, to Display all memos / payments for a financial year in one page, or to break it up into pages of a custom number of items." Then: "Sign out should also ALWAYS be a logo" and "In 'Statistics', move 'Edit Statistics' to Settings > Start Years & Statistics".

- Every financial year box is a drop-down of `YYYY-YY` labels, newest first (`fees/services/fy_options.py`, `templates/fees/_fy_select.html`). Filters on Memos, Drafts, Feebook and Statistics list the years the profile has memos in plus the current year, never earlier than the area's start year (Memos, Feebook or Statistics start year), and always keep the year already chosen. Memos, Drafts and Feebook add "All years"; the Explore tab adds "Earliest" and "Latest". The Settings start-year drop-downs run from the earliest year with data (ten years back when there is none) to the current year. The `fy`, `from_fy` and `to_fy` query names and values are unchanged.
- With no `fy` in the link, Memos, Drafts and Feebook open on the current financial year when it has rows, else on the newest financial year that has rows, else on All years; an explicit `fy` (including an explicit current year or a blank All years) is always kept (`filter_options.default_year`).
- New setting `items_per_page` (Settings > Other > Display), default 50. 0 or "All" puts everything on one page; otherwise the page holds that many rows, at most 1000. It applies to Memos, Drafts and Feebook; totals and footers always cover the whole filtered set. Outstanding does not page. The Excel backup carries it in the `WebSettings` sheet (web-only key); an older file restores to 50.
- Edit Statistics (working days, extra counts and replacements) is no longer a Statistics tab; it sits in Settings > Start Years & Statistics. `?tab=edit` on the Statistics page now shows the Standard tab. Saving a month returns to that Settings tab. The save route `fees_settings_statistics_grid` is unchanged.
- Log out on the Home page is always the icon, in Words mode too.
- Test changes: `fees.tests.test_ui_pages` (the Edit grid tests now use Settings), `fees.tests.test_ui_screens` and `fees.tests.test_round2_views` (the `?tab=edit` entries now point at Settings), `accounts.tests.test_header_style.test_words_on_the_home_page` (Log out stays an icon), the `WebSettings` key list in `fees.tests.test_excel_backup`.


## Round 22: Words by default; Search, Home and Sign out always icons

Owner instruction, verbatim: "Words / Logo should default to Words. Search and Home should ALWAYS be logos." Then: "Sign out should also ALWAYS be a logo".

- With no stored choice (`SuiteName` rows `brand_home`, `brand_diary`, `brand_fees`) the header bar shows Words for Home, the Case Law Diary and the Fee Register (`accounts/suite.py`, `accounts/context_processors.py`, the Page Names form). A choice an Admin has already saved is kept.
- Search, Home and Sign out are icons whatever the choice, with an accessible label (`aria-label` and `title`): `templates/_search_button.html`, `templates/_home_button.html`, `templates/_signout_button.html`. The choice still applies to the other header items (Trash, Flags, Export, Help, Settings, Admins and so on).
- Sign out (was "Log out", and only on the Home page) is now in every header. No extra query: the choice still comes from the one `suite.read_all` query.
- Test changes: tests that check the icons choose Logo first (`accounts.tests.test_header_style`, and the header tests in `diary.tests.test_views`, `diary.tests.test_importer_db`, `accounts.tests.test_round17`); `test_header_style` has a new test for the Words default.


## Round 22: uniform header on every page

Owner instruction, verbatim: "The format of the Header should be uniform in all pages, i.e., user name, search and Home should be on the right, with the rest on the left."

- One shell (`templates/base_suite.html`, which Home, Admins, Settings, Help, the Case Law Diary, the Fee Register and the login pages all extend) draws the header, so every page has the same format. LEFT: program brand (and profile switcher), Back / Forward, then the program's own items. RIGHT, in this order: the signed-in user's name (a link to Settings > Account), Search, Home, and Sign out at the far right (`<div class="header-end">`; partials `_header_user.html`, `_search_button.html`, `_home_button.html`, `_signout_button.html`).
- Search is shown where the page has a search (the Case Law Diary with a profile, and the Fee Register); the suite pages and the login page have none, so it is left out there. The login page shows only the brand: nobody is signed in, so there is no name, Home or Sign out.
- Home now also appears on the Home page itself (it used to show only Log out there), and the user name now also shows inside the Case Law Diary and the Fee Register.
- Accessibility is unchanged: the skip link, the "Main" navigation landmark, labels on every icon. Layout: `static/css/design.css` (`.header-end`) and `static/css/look.css`; the right-hand group wraps under the nav on a phone (360 to 390 px), with a long name cut short with an ellipsis. Profile switcher, Words / Logo, Links toggle, the Fee Register search drop-down and the diary Alt+F search bar behave as before.
- Test changes: the markup assertions in `diary.tests.test_views` (name on every page, Home on the Home page, Sign out in every header) and `accounts.tests.test_accounts` (the name is a link).


## Round: settings IA and filter rules

Owner instruction, verbatim: "Yes to all three of your points. Further, let 'My settings' be accessibly be clicking on the user-name in the header. Further: 1. Drop down menus should only show the firms, etc, available in the present filter view. 2. In all drop down menus where I can select options, give me an option to select all / none. 3. All drop-down menus with a year should show the current financial year by default. 4. Give me a complete list of which settings are per user and which are per profile."

Approved plan (three places):

- My settings: only you. Reached by clicking the user name in the header.
- Program settings: a Settings tab inside each program (Case Law Diary, Fee Register). Editors and Admins can change it; Readers see it read-only.
- Admin: Users, Profiles, Appearance and Backups merged into one area.

Owner answers to the open questions:

- Opening page and Items per page become personal (per user) in both programs.
- View whole subject stays per profile.
- My edits moves into the diary menu and is renamed History.

The four numbered rules, to be applied across the suite:

1. Drop-down menus show only the firms, and so on, that are available in the present filter view.
2. Every drop-down where options can be selected offers select all / none.
3. Every drop-down with a year shows the current financial year by default.
4. A complete list of which settings are per user and which are per profile is published (a table in the settings documentation).

Where settings live today (baseline, before this round):

- Per user, suite-wide (`accounts.User`): `landing_program`, `last_diary_path`, `header_style`, `justify_text`.
- Per user, Case Law Diary (`diary.UserSettings`): `opening_page`, `opening_subject`, the five numbering formats, `export_date_format` (`export_custom_name` and `time_zone` removed, D163).
- Per user and profile (`fees.FeesUserPrefs`, JSON by key): interface state such as resized column widths.
- Per profile (`accounts.Profile`): `name`, `export_name`, `display_name` (Home Page Name), `header_name` (Header Name: the program header; hovering shows the Home Page Name; migration `accounts/0025`, old backups restore it blank), `reading_all_default`, `reading_all_mode`.
- Per profile (`fees.FeesSettings`): memo prefills, `overdue_days`, the financial year starts, money display, `hide_payment_when_paid`, `default_lines_enabled`, `default_payment_amount`, and the other Fee Register keys.
- Suite-wide (`accounts.SuiteName`): the page names and brand choices.

## Round: Title Case for control labels, All Memos, Columns button placement

Owner instruction, verbatim: "The names of all tabs, sub-tabs, buttons, Filter options should be First Letter Capitalised for All Words. In Outstanding, rename the button Memo by Memo as All Memos. When I enter that memo By Memo View, the Columns button is poorly aligned, and partly overlaps with the table."

Decisions:

- Every word of the visible name of a tab, sub-tab, header item, button, filter label and drop-down option starts with a capital, including of, and, to, on, the and a ("Include Written Off Memos", "View Overdue Memos Only", "All Years", "Active Only", "Amount Due (High To Low)", "Reset Columns & Widths"). Abbreviations keep their form (TDS, PAN, FY, Excel, Word, IST, No.).
- Not changed: data values, page body text, help text, content headings and table column headers.
- Outstanding: the button that opens the one-memo-per-row list is now "All Memos". The URL and its `view` values are unchanged.
- The Columns control sits in a toolbar above the table (`.table-tools`), right aligned, with clear space above and below. A negative bottom margin on that toolbar pulled the table up under the button; it is now a normal margin, so the button never overlaps the table at desktop or phone width. This is central CSS (`static/css/ledger.css`), so every table with a Columns button gets it.

### Title Case for model choice labels

Owner decision: "every word First Letter Capitalised". The model-level choice labels shown as options now follow it too, by hand-written `AlterField` migrations (`accounts` 0024, `diary` 0023); stored values are unchanged. Abbreviations keep their form.

- `diary.UserSettings.opening_page`: "The Last Page I Viewed", "A Chosen Subject" (Home, Other Subjects, Global Search were already in Title Case).
- `accounts.Profile.reading_all_mode`: "The Whole Subject", "Only That Topic Or Sub-topic", "The View I Used Last".
- `diary.Item.citation_layout`: "On Separate Lines" (shown as an option on the Item card and editor).
- Left as they are: `diary.Item/Section.flag` (single words, already capitalised), `kind`, number formats (symbols), date formats, `accounts` role and header style (single words), `backups` kinds (not shown as options).

## Round: payments over any number of memos
Owner instruction, verbatim: "Allow me to add more than 5 Memos per payment. Also, when adding multiple Memos per payment, (1) create some division between the various Memos that I am adding, (ii) let it look more like the attached screenshot, where I get to see a drop down menu with Memos, and it tells me the amount due there."
- A payment can be split over any number of memos. The old limit of 5 rows (and of 5 `?memo=` values) is gone. One cap, `payments.MAX_ALLOCATIONS` = 200 (the same as the rest of the system), is used everywhere: the payments service, the payment form (rows, Add Another Memo, `?memo=` prefill) and the Outstanding Pay Ticked Memos bar and its All button, which read it from the page (the bar script had its own hard-coded 5 before). With the script, Pay Ticked Memos sends one comma-separated `memo=`.
- The "Allocate To Memos" section shows each memo as its own block with its row number. The form starts with three rows. "+ Memo" (top right, needs the script) adds a row and "x" removes one. Without the script, a plain "Add Another Memo" button adds an empty row on the server and saves nothing.
- Each row's Memo is a drop-down whose options read "2026-27/178 · Matter Name (Rs 60,000 Due)". The options are the profile's numbered non-draft memos with an amount due that are not written off, newest first, plus memos already on the payment and memos named in `?memo=`. When the form was opened from a memo, that memo's matter and firm are listed first. The list is read in one query and rendered in every row.
- Picking a memo fills that row's Amount with the amount due (at most what is left of the payment) and the TDS from the row's Rate %. Each row has a Rate % (starting at the payment's TDS rate) and an auto tick for its TDS; both are conveniences in the page, the server still works out and checks every stored figure.

## Round: who is active

Owner: "Create a toggle, for each User, which will decide whether or not they can see which users / profiles are presently active. If allowed, display this on the HOME page."

- `User.can_see_active` ("Can See Who Is Active", default off) is set by an Admin on Admin > Users (create and edit). An Admin always sees the panel, whatever the toggle (an Admin is an Admin everywhere).
- `User.last_seen_at` is written by `accounts.middleware.LastSeenMiddleware` for signed-in requests, at most once every 60 seconds per user, by one UPDATE that runs only when the value on `request.user` is empty or stale (so most page views add no query).
- Home shows "Active Now" (seen in the last 5 minutes) and "Earlier Today", newest first, each with the user's name (display name, else username), program and profile, taken from their most recently used live membership (`ProfileMembership.last_used_at`). Two queries, and only for a permitted viewer. The data is on the Home page only, never in a JSON endpoint.

## Round: sortable Admin tables and resizable columns everywhere

Owner: "In Admin > Users / Profiles, allow me to sort the Users / Profiles by each column, and also to resize the columns. I want to be able to resize EVERY column in the program - tell me which columns you will have to change."

- **Sorting.** Every column heading of Admin > Users and Admin > Profiles is a link: first click ascending, second click descending, an arrow shows the current sort and the heading carries `aria-sort`. It is done on the server with `?sort=<column>` (`-` in front means descending), so it works without JavaScript. Only listed columns are accepted (Users: username, admin, profiles, active, last_login; Profiles: name, program, display_name, users, subjects); an unknown value is ignored and the default (name, ascending) is used. Ties always fall back to the name, ascending, in both directions. Computed columns (the profiles and roles text, the counts) are sorted in Python after they are worked out. The Deleted Users, Deleted Profiles and removal log tables are not sorted (they are short and already newest first).
- **Resizing.** One shared script, `static/js/resizable-columns.js`, now does all column resizing; `static/js/fees/columns.js` keeps only the Fee Register's Columns show and hide menu, its Reset button, server storage of widths, and calls the shared script for the handles. It loads once, from `templates/base_suite.html`, ahead of the page scripts. Every `<table>` in `templates/` has a stable `data-table-id` (a static test fails if one is added without it). Tables whose first heading row uses colspan are left alone, and a table with no `<thead>` (a two-column list of names and values) takes its handles on the first row.
- **Where widths are kept.** Fee Register tables: on the server per user and profile (as before). Everything else: this browser's localStorage `quill.cols.<id>`. See docs/SETTINGS.md.
- **Columns changed.** The full list (page, table, id, whether it was already resizable, what changed) was reported to the owner with this round; Home and Settings contain no tables, so there is nothing to resize there.

## Round: no Attach menu on Feebook rows

Owner: "In feebook, for an unaccounted memo, remove the tab to 'Attach Memo number' - if I have to add a memo number, I will do it from 'Edit'."

- The "..." More actions menu (Memo number box and Attach button) is gone from every Feebook row. The row keeps its Edit button; the payment edit form allocates the payment to memos. The Memo column still shows plain "Unaccounted".
- The `fees_payment_assign` route and view are unchanged (nothing on the Feebook links to it now). The `.row-menu` CSS and the row-menu overlay code in `static/js/fees/columns.js` were removed; the Columns menu (`.col-menu`) is unaffected.
- Test fix: the Feebook unaccounted page was listed as a page that must show a `status-sq` marker, but its only status is the plain "Unaccounted" text (no marker, by the owner rule). It now sits with the pages checked only with markers off.

## Page titles that repeat the header or the sub-tab (owner rule)

Owner: "pages need not have their names displayed at the top of the page, as the names would already be displayed either in the Header bar or in the sub-tab bar, underlined, and which makes it clear what we are looking at."

The orchestrator proposed removing such titles, keeping the title where it is the only visible name, and treating icon-only header entries as a condition on the Words / Logo choice. Owner: "Go ahead with your suggestions".

How it is built:

- Every page keeps exactly one `<h1>` for screen readers. Where the visible title is removed the `<h1>` gets the existing `visually-hidden` class (the `<title>` element is unchanged). Buttons that shared the title row (for example Export To Excel, New Memo) stay in the row, right-aligned (`static/css/ledger.css`).
- The decision is made in each template from the nav data, with no extra query: unconditional where the underlined entry is always words (Fee Register header entries, and every sub-tab), or `header_words` / `home_header_words` where the entry is an icon under Logo.

Visually hidden (the underlined header entry or sub-tab says the same):

- Fee Register: Statistics and its Dashboard, Standard and Explore tabs (not when Statistics is switched off, where the notice page keeps its title); Memos; Draft memos; Firms; Feebook; Settings.
- Admins: Users, Profiles, Appearance, Backups (Saved Backups and Email Backups are sections of that page).
- Settings: My settings (when the Settings sub-tab bar shows).
- Case Law Diary: Flags and Hyperlinks.

Visually hidden only when the header shows words (kept when icons are shown): Home "Programs" (Home choice), Case Law Diary Trash, History, Export, Settings (Case Law Diary settings, Reading and Data sections) and Help (Home choice for Help).

Kept visible (the title is the only visible name): Fee Register Outstanding by firm and Outstanding memos (the tab says "Outstanding"), memo, firm, matter and payment pages and forms, ledger, statement, Move Memo, Year End, Search, Backup, Import, Restore and Clear pages; the diary Index of Subjects, Subject pages, editors, Move, Link and Confirm pages, conflict pages, item history and versions, Trash sub-pages, Import from Word, Offline Link, and Search (the magnifier is always an icon); Admin user and profile forms, delete, clear and restore pages; Password change; sign-in, forgot password, error and busy pages.

- **Home lists only the profiles given to a user; Admin Profile Access for the rest.** [Owner: a profile is active for a user only when explicitly made active; an Admin, who can reach every profile, opens the others from a separate page.] Home shows a button for each Case Law Diary and Fee Register profile given to the user on the Users screen (the one in use is a link, the others switch to it). Admins > **Profile Access** (`admin_profile_access`, Admin only) lists every live profile of every program that is on, with Open buttons. Opening a profile not given to the Admin records the choice in a membership marked `ProfileMembership.admin_access` (migration `accounts/0026`); such memberships are not on Home and not ticked on the Users screen, and giving the profile on the Users screen clears the mark. **Active Now** lists every profile a user worked in today (`ProfileMembership.last_seen_at`, set by `accounts.middleware` at most once a minute for the profile a `/caselaw/` or `/fees/` page used).

## Investments program (plan: design/investments/INVESTMENTS_PLAN.md)

Decisions taken while building the Investments program. Where the plan was silent, ambiguous or contradictory the builder implemented the stated default and recorded it here; the pull request lists every such point for the owner.

- **D102. Investments is a third program behind `INVESTMENTS_ENABLED`.** Same rules as `FEES_ENABLED` (unset: on in developer mode and tests, off in production; `render.yaml` ships `"0"`). `accounts/programs.py` `is_enabled` now knows both switchable programs. URL names start with `investments_` and there is no URL namespace, so `current_program_key` finds the program from the name prefix, as for the Fee Register. The Appearance (Page Names) screen gets an Investments name field that may be left out of a post (an older client keeps the current name).
- **D103. Page shells.** Every page of `design/investments/mockups/index.html` is a row of `investments/pages.py` and is served as an empty shell with its empty-state text until its phase replaces it. Navigation is five top entries (Overview, Holdings, Reports, Alerts, Settings), each with an optional second row, as in the mockups (plan 25.2 says "about six" but lists five). Page titles were visible, as in the mockups; superseded by D141, which applies the Fee Register rule.
- **D104. Auto / Full number display (plan 24.3).** `investments/money_auto.py` with a BigInt JavaScript mirror and one golden table. The plan left these open; chosen: rounding is half away from zero on the exact amount; a figure that rounds up to the next unit is shown in that unit (999.995 is `₹1.00 k`, 99.999 lac is `₹1.00 cr`); a negative uses a true minus before the symbol (`−₹60.30 k`) and a gain may carry `+`; zero shows no sign; Full shows whole rupees without decimals and an amount with paise with two (`₹950.50`), matching the mockups; Auto below 1,000 behaves the same way; foreign amounts use k and m in Auto and Western grouping in Full. Units are k from 1,000, lac from 1,00,000 and cr from 1,00,00,000 (so 99,999.50 is `₹1.00 lac`, not `100.00 k`).
- **D105. Registry placeholders.** `backups/registry.py` has `INVESTMENTS_BACKUP_MODELS` and `INVESTMENT_PROFILE_MODELS` (and `PROGRAM_PROFILE_MODELS["investments"]`); `investments/apps.py` reads the second list lazily. A test keeps the two views identical. `profile_usage` counts an Investments profile's transactions (`investments/services/usage.py`) so the "refuse to delete a profile in use" rule applies.
- **D106. Units, lots and the selling limit (plan 6).** `investments/lots.py` (pure Python) replays a profile's final transactions and transfers per investment. A bucket is (portfolio, broker, folio or none); a sale draws only on the bucket it names, FIFO inside it, so it can never exceed the units owned in total, with the broker, in the folio, or with both. Purchases count before sales on the same date. A purchase's units exist from its **allotment date** when one is recorded, else from the date paid (so a sale between the two dates cannot use them; the plan is silent, CA to confirm, plan 3.17); the date paid still starts XIRR and "amount invested". Every add, edit, delete, completion, transfer and restore replays the whole history of the investment and is refused, with the conflicting later entries listed, if any bucket would go negative on any date. A refusal is the 409 page "This change is refused".
- **D107. Cost and proceeds.** Charges are added to a purchase's cost and deducted from a sale's proceeds (plan 3.3, proposed default), and a sale's charges are spread over the lots it consumes in proportion to units. `cost_override` and its note on a purchase replace its total cost (grandfathering, plan 3.2, proposed); it is stored now and used by the Annual Report later.
- **D108. XIRR (plan 16.5).** Newton from 10 percent, kept inside -99 percent to +1,000 percent, with bisection when Newton leaves the bracket. "n/a" for fewer than two flows, flows all of one sign, no root in range, or a total period under 30 days (the plan says "too short to annualise" without a number; 30 is the assumption). A holding's XIRR counts what is held (and awaiting purchases at cost) as money received on the as-at date.
- **D109. Awaiting allotment (plan 6).** Status `awaiting`, `amount` (the amount paid or the expected proceeds), no units or price. Counted at cost from the date paid (or left out, by Settings), never in FIFO, limits, average cost or sales. Completing needs any two of units, NAV and the amount (the amount is already known); a difference up to the larger of Rs 1 and 0.1 percent of the amount is rounding, more needs a tick. The date paid stays, the allotment date is recorded and starts the holding period. An awaiting redemption is not checked against what is held until it is completed.
- **D110. Where records live.** Shared: `Instrument` (null profile) and `ManualPrice` (a hand-entered price, backed up; the fetched `Price` table of the price phase is never backed up). Private to a profile: alternative investments (`Instrument.profile` set), everything else. Instrument kinds: share (ETFs too), fund, alternative, foreign, index. An index cannot be bought. The built-in indices are created by a data migration. A look-alike investment (same code, or same name apart from case and spaces) is offered instead of a copy. Editors may add shared investments (the Admin catalogue arrives in a later phase); only an Admin changes shared details.
- **D111. Edit history (plan 16.4).** `InvestmentHistory` holds a full snapshot for every create, edit, delete, cancel, completion and restore of a transaction, transfer, switch, folio number and alternative value. Nothing in the program deletes or edits it, and it is not the diary's `VersionHistory`, so the nightly purge never touches it (tested). A deleted or cancelled entry is restored with its old id after the whole history is re-checked.
- **D112. Backup registry.** All Investments tables except `InvestmentsUserPrefs` are in `BACKUP_MODELS`, parents first, and optional; `INVESTMENT_PROFILE_MODELS` drives the profile purge and clear (children first). A restore replaces every table with `.delete()`, so once fetched prices exist a restore empties them and they refill from the nightly job (plan 17.2 says the same). New backup kinds `pre_inv_delete` and `pre_catalogue` (Admin may delete them like the import backups).
- **D113. Per-person display choice.** The Auto / Full choice is saved per person (`InvestmentsUserPrefs`, not backed up), starting from the profile's Settings choice. Reading a page never writes a row: settings, a first portfolio and the like are created by the first Editor action.
- **D114. Holdings values (plan 5, 11).** An investment with no price is valued at cost and tagged "No price"; an alternative investment is valued from its latest dated value (unchanged or averaged between entries, by Settings) and at cost before the first value; a foreign investment is valued with the rupee rate of its latest transaction until exchange rates are fetched. Index comparison cards show a dash until index prices exist. With a broker filter, a transfer is not counted as a cash flow (it will be, at market value, when the Compare brokers report is built).
- **D115. Prices (plan 15, 16.1, 16.2, 24.5).** `investments/adapters/` (`urllib` only; `yahoo`, `amfi`, `mfapi`) behind `services/fetch.py`, which the nightly `fetch_prices`, the Update Prices button and the Admin re-fetch all use. A short `Mozilla/5.0` User-Agent (a long one was refused from Render, plan 30), retries of 429, 5xx and network errors with a doubling delay plus up to a second of randomness (three tries), requests spaced about a second apart, prices of zero or below rejected, only the days since the last stored date requested, a run-time cap and a symbol cap. A failed symbol keeps its last price and is recorded in `SourceRun`; nothing is looped. Fetched prices go to `Price` (never in the suite backup); a hand-entered price (`ManualPrice`) wins over a fetched one for the same day; an `upload` row is never replaced by a fetched one. New shares and indices get the `split_adjusted` basis, funds none. The AMFI file address is the setting `INVESTMENTS_AMFI_URL` (default `https://www.amfiindia.com/spages/NAVAll.txt`), because its final address is not yet confirmed (plan 3.9, 30); an AMFI answer with none of the schemes asked for is an error, never silence. Funds get their history once from mfapi.in, then the daily AMFI file. A new split reported by Yahoo is stored as a `SplitSignal`; for a split-adjusted instrument the whole fetched history is replaced (uploaded rows are kept and counted), once per split (plan 16.2, still to be confirmed on a real split).
- **D116. Update Prices (plan 24.5).** `POST /investments/prices/update/` with a step number: step 0 does all funds together (one AMFI download), later steps ten other symbols each, each step inside `heavy_operation("price_update")`; the page loops and shows progress. A new run is refused for about a minute after the last one started; a second person gets "an update is in progress". The status line reads the last run, the newest price date and the number not updated.
- **D117. History upload (plan 29).** `services/price_import.py`: CSV, Excel (the Fee Register's streaming reader) or a price-archive zip; columns found by name; a dry run first (counts per investment, unknown symbols skipped, gaps over ten days, one-day moves over 40 percent, duplicates, differences from stored prices) then an all-or-nothing apply. Plan 29.4 lists bad dates, non-positive prices, future dates and a differing basis as things to "reject", while also saying unknown symbols are "skipped": the choice made is that a bad row blocks the whole file (so "all-or-nothing per file" means the file), and unknown symbols are skipped. The dry run assumes the rows of one investment are in date order (it keeps only the previous row), and says so when they are not. The page stages the file in the temporary directory for an hour between the two steps. Two further departures: plan 29.5 says to stream with the Fee Register reader, but that reader stops at 50,000 rows and at 10 MB, which cannot hold a ten-year history of even a few dozen investments, so CSV and archive files are read by the program's own streaming reader (Excel files still use the Fee Register reader and its 50,000-row cap); and the web page refuses files over 3 MB (about 100,000 rows; the dry run and the apply of 60,000 rows took about 18 seconds on a laptop, so more would pass the 60-second request limit) and points to `import_prices`.
- **D118. Catalogue (plan 21.1).** `services/catalogue.py` and the Settings > Catalogue pages, Admin only. Holders are shown as profile names; nothing from a transaction is ever on those pages (tested). An edited or added price becomes a hand-entered price and the original goes to the permanent `CatalogueHistory`; a deleted price can be put back from the log; editing a pinned date updates the pin. Merge, price-basis change and delete take a `pre_catalogue` backup first and are refused if it cannot be made. The plan's merge refusal for "a holding that cannot be combined without losing a broker or folio distinction" has no case that can occur (a merge only adds units to buckets and equal folios are joined), so only a different type, a different currency or a private investment refuse a merge. A shared investment cannot be deleted while any profile has records for it.
- **D119. Pins (plan 17.2).** `services/pins.py`. A pin is made from stored prices only. It is `approximate` when the stored price is more than five days before the day asked for. When a transaction is saved the pin is made from what is stored, without any request to a price service (the plan also says to fetch the day if it is missing; that is left to the nightly `pin_prices` step so a page save never waits on the internet). As-of lookups check a pin for the exact date first, then stored prices, then the nearest earlier pin (approximate).
- **D120. Pruning, archive and its email (plan 17.3 to 17.5).** `services/pruning.py`, `services/archive.py`. `prune_prices` is the last link of the cron `&&` chain and also checks today's backup itself; it leaves alone any instrument with a missing required pin, deletes in chunks of 5,000 with a time limit, and exits 0 when off or declining. The archive is made when asked, streamed and deleted, with the same columns as the upload plus a `pins.csv` and a manifest; it is split into parts on instrument boundaries; restoring is the same dry-run-then-apply upload (`import_prices --archive`). The mailer has a fifth kind, `archive`, built one part at a time and always sent in emails of its own, each within `max_attachment_mb` even when "split emails" is off; more than five parts means nothing is sent and the warning bar says so. A pruned live database always gives a pruned archive.
- **D121. Index comparison and Performance (plan 5, 9).** `investments/indexcmp.py` (pure): every purchase buys index units at that day's level and every sale sells them; the index XIRR is the XIRR of those flows with the remaining units valued at the end date; Alpha is the difference. A missing level gives n/a, never a guess. Levels: a pin for the exact date, else the last stored close on or before it (the close before the window answers its first days); a level more than a week old, or an approximate pin, adds a note. Performance: the value on the start date is the opening investment; an investment bought later starts at its first purchase and says so; CAGR is the investment's own price growth from its first to its last stored price in the period (none for alternative investments). The sectoral columns exist only while the switch is on. The Include Dividends switch is stored but has nothing to include until dividends are recorded (Phase 4).
- **D122. Charts and Total Portfolio Value (plan 9 "Charts", 12).** Server-side sampling: daily up to three months, weekly up to three years, month-ends beyond, at most 400 points and 8 lines; prices are read one instrument at a time. Chart A's portfolio line is a time-weighted index (money added in a period counts from its start), so deposits do not make it jump; index and Chart B lines are rebased to 100 at their first priced point. Lines differ by dash pattern as well as shade, and each chart has a table (the last 60 points on the page, all in the Excel file). The Dashboard chart is the portfolio's value against the same money put into the Default Index. Total Portfolio Value on any date splits by type, portfolio and broker (each split is an exact re-computation with that filter, not a share of the total) and never includes dividends.
- **D123. Cache (plan 26.4).** `InvestmentsStatsCache` holds the Dashboard figures, chart data and the value timeline, keyed by profile, kind and a hash of the parameters, valid while the profile's `data_version` and the global price version both match (and the date, for today's figures); at most 200 rows per profile; not backed up. Money travels through it as text.
- **D124. Dividends (plan 7).** Recorded by the user (amount received after tax, tax deducted, broker, optional folio; foreign amounts optional) and logged in the edit history. Never part of any value. The Include Dividends switch on the Dashboard is remembered for the profile when an Editor changes it (a Reader's choice applies to the page only); when on, each dividend counts as money received on its date in every XIRR, the index replay takes the same flows, and the page says the comparison is against the price index only. The price source's dividend history (Yahoo `events=div`) is kept as `DividendSignal` (not backed up) for a suggested amount (per unit times units held the day before the ex-date) and for "may be due" reminders (60 days). Mutual fund IDCW payouts are entered the same way (plan 3.4).
- **D125. Corporate actions (plan 8, 3.7 proposed default).** One shared `CorporateAction` (split "1 becomes 5", bonus "1 free for every 2", other) and one `CorporateActionResponse` per profile (applied, not applicable). Only applied actions enter the lot arithmetic: on the ex-date, before that day's trades, a split multiplies every lot's units (cost and dates kept) and a bonus adds a zero-cost lot dated the ex-date in each bucket (its own holding period; fractions are kept, cash in lieu is not modelled; CA to confirm). An unconfirmed action dated after the profile's first purchase makes the holding Provisional with a banner, or, with "block", n/a and out of the totals (section 2). Taking an applied action off re-checks the history. A split found in Yahoo's split history is recorded automatically as a pending action for every holder. A one-day fall of more than 35 percent with no action within five days is listed as "Possible split? Check." Recording an action on a split-adjusted instrument divides its earlier pins by the factor once (plan 17.2). Past valuations on a split-adjusted series multiply the units then by every applied action after that day (plan 16.2); raw series use units as they were. "Other" (merger, demerger) changes nothing automatically; the lots are corrected with the cost override.
- **D126. Alerts (plan 14).** Worked out when a page is opened and cached until the data or prices change. Rule A: latest close against the one before, and optionally against the close N days ago (default fall 5, rise 8, 15 percent over 7 days); Rule B: value against the FIFO cost of units still held, per portfolio (default 20 below, 40 above). Guardrails per investment and portfolio; by default a guardrail replaces the global rules for that holding. An alert shows the price date, price, gain, short or long term and days to long-term. Dismiss hides it until a new price date; snooze until a date; re-arm (for percentage alerts) raises the level it must pass. Alternative investments use their last value (Rule B only) and a maturity reminder (default 30 days). The proposed daily alert email is built and off by default (`send_alert_emails`, one email per profile with new alerts, each alert once per price date). The Alerts menu entry shows the count.
- **D127. Annual Report and capital gains (plan 13).** `services/annual.py`. A completed year is as at 31 March, the current one as at today; only the label (for example 2025-26) is shown. Starting value at the 31 March before, ending value at the closing date (Total Portfolio Value, with pins for exactly 31 March winning over stored prices, so the report is the same after a restore with no daily prices: tested). Amount invested and sold use the date paid; awaiting purchases count at cost and make the report Provisional. Gains are first-in-first-out over each investment's whole history; a sale is long-term when held **more** than the year's cutoff in days (the plan does not say "more than" or "at least"; "more than" matches the usual 12-month rule; CA to confirm). The cutoff is chosen per year on the report and stored in `InvestmentSettings.year_cutoffs`; Settings holds the default for later years. The per-lot cost override replaces a purchase's cost. Bonus lots carry zero cost. The year's XIRR treats the starting value as money put in on 31 March. The index return is the Default Index's change over the year. Excel export: summary, capital gains, holdings at year end, transactions, by portfolio. Fractions from bonuses are kept (no cash in lieu).
- **D128. One investment's page (plan 10).** A date picker shows the price and value on any earlier day. Purchase by purchase: each lot still held with its gain, its return (CAGR for a year or more, plain return under a year) next to the Default Index over the same dates and the difference, and the days until it turns long-term; the sold portions with their realised result. The price chart marks purchases and sales. The 52-week range comes from stored prices. The next results date is a field an Admin can set (Current PE was removed, D145) in the catalogue: Yahoo's chart answer has no PE, and the plan's sources offer none, so PE is "not available" unless typed (gap listed for the owner).
- **D129. Foreign investments (plan 22).** Units, price and charges in the investment's currency; the rupee amount uses the day's stored rate unless the rate or the rupee amount is typed (a missing rate is refused with that advice). Rates are fetched from Yahoo as `{CUR}INR=X` by the nightly job (`FxRate`, not backed up) with hand-entered `ManualFxRate` (backed up) winning; the last rate on or before a day is used. The value uses the current rate; the investment page shows value, cost and gain in both currencies and the currency effect. Capital gains are in rupees (rupee cost and rupee proceeds). Charts and alerts in local currency are not built (gap: plan 22 "Charts: a switch", "Alerts on the local price").
- **D130. SIP schedules (plan 6).** Amount, day of month (a short month uses its last day), portfolio, broker, optional folio, start and optional end. `create_sip_entries` makes each due month's entry from the first NAV stored on or after the SIP date within a week (the transaction date stays the SIP date; the NAV date is the allotment date); if a month's NAV is not there yet that month and later ones wait. Entries are pending by default and confirmed on To complete; the Settings choice makes final entries.
- **D131. Statement import (plan 6, 27).** One general adapter that finds the columns by name, using the Fee Register's streaming reader and its date and number parsers; broker-specific layouts need sample files (gap). Investments are matched on ISIN, code (also with .NS added), scheme code or exact name; unknown ones must be added first. A review shows what each line will do; saving is all or nothing in date order. A purchase line that matches an awaiting entry (same investment, date within 10 days, amount within ten times the completion tolerance) completes it; a line identical to a stored trade (investment, date, side, units) is skipped as a duplicate.
- **D132. Search, insights and reports (plan 24.2, 5, 9).** Search follows the Fee Register's rules (two characters, literal wildcards) over investments, folios, brokers, transactions and visible pages. Insights are six fixed rules (portfolio against the index with the leading holding; a holding 15 percent or more below cost and trailing the index; purchases turning long-term within 60 days; a holding above the concentration limit, default 20 percent; dividends this year against the same date last year; old prices), ranked, dismissable, each kind switchable in Settings > Insights, top five on the Dashboard. Other reports: year-by-year, best and worst, index scoreboard, dividends with yield on cost, income, lots turning long-term, unrealised by lot, concentration, cost of funds (expense ratio entered by an Admin), data check, compare portfolios, compare brokers. With a portfolio or broker filter, a transfer across the boundary counts as money leaving one side and arriving at the other at the market value that day.
- **D133. Adding an investment (plan 6).** Shares and foreign: Yahoo's symbol search when it answers, otherwise the code is typed; before saving, the latest price is fetched and shown (name, exchange, price date) for the person to confirm. Funds: AMFI's whole scheme list is stored (`AmfiScheme`, not backed up) and refreshed weekly by the nightly job, so the exact plan and option is picked by name or code. Twelve Data was tried as a fallback source and removed (its free plan does not cover Indian markets; investments 0028 moves its instruments to the default source for their kind). Shadow mode and the comparison report for switching vendors (plan 15.7) are not built (gap).
- **D134. Alternative investments (plan 11).** Dated values with the unchanged or averaged treatment (a default in Settings, a switch on Total Portfolio Value), or a fixed interest rate compounded yearly from each purchase when no dated value exists; a maturity reminder. Private to the profile.
- **D135.
