Local helper (plan 28).** Built and off: `PRICE_HELPER_ENABLED` (environment, read per request) makes `GET /investments/api/prices/wanted/` and `POST /investments/api/prices/ingest/` real; otherwise both are a 404 and the Price sources page says only "Local helper: off". Tokens (only a SHA-256 hash stored, shown once, revocable, not backed up, 120 calls an hour), HTTPS only (allowed over HTTP in developer mode), per-source fetch mode (server, helper, either; a "helper" source is skipped by the server's own fetch). Ingest uses the history upload's validation, writes source `helper` inside `heavy_operation("price_ingest")`, never over a hand-entered or uploaded price, and records a `SourceRun`. `tools/price_helper.py` and `tools/bhavcopy_to_csv.py` (both standard library only) and `docs/PRICE_HELPER.md` with a launchd sample. The plan's "Last helper upload" in the status line is on the Price sources page only.
- **D136. Measure and tune (plan 26 and 31).** The memory bench covers every guarded operation of plan 26.1 and fails if one is over its cap. The Annual Report reads all lot events once (`core.events_by_instrument`) and shares them with the per-portfolio breakdown. With one portfolio, that row reuses the report's own figures, and each sub-report keeps only its summary figures. Performance cuts the opening holdings down to (units, value) before the closing holdings are computed. Alerts read the recent closes of every holding with one query per price table. Results are in `docs/INVESTMENTS_RAM.md`: all within cap, with an RSS peak of 120.6 MB in the bench. The Annual Report is still the CPU-heavy page (about 5 s here). Caching it per data version is the next step if Render's 0.5 CPU makes it too slow; it is not built yet.
- **D137. Fee Register: Dashboard and Bank switches.** [Owner (2026-11): "Also have an option to turn off dashboard entirely and also shut off showing 'Bank' anywhere (including the option to filter for it on Feebook, the column in Feebook and the option to add it in Payments)."] Two per-profile settings on `FeesSettings` (migration `fees/0006`), both on by default and changed by Editors like the other switches. They are in the Fee Register backup (`WebSettings`).
  - **`dashboard_enabled`** (later merged into `statistics_enabled`, see D163; the notes below describe what the one switch does when off). When it is off:
    - The header entry opens Statistics. If Statistics is also off, the entry is left out.
    - The Statistics tabs have no Dashboard tab, and the program name links to the first page.
    - `/fees/dashboard/` redirects to the landing page, and an Opening page of Dashboard opens Memos instead. Dashboard is not offered as an Opening page.
    - The KPI JSON still answers.
  - **`show_bank`** (Settings > Payments, "Show bank"). When it is off, the following are hidden:
    - the Feebook Bank filter and column;
    - the payment form's Bank account box;
    - the Feebook export's Bank column;
    - the Bank accounts list in Settings.

    A `bank` in an old Feebook link is ignored, so nothing is filtered unseen. Editing a payment keeps the bank it had: the view supplies the stored bank instead of the missing box, which would otherwise have cleared it. Recorded banks and the bank accounts are kept and backed up.
- **D138. Links icon, Home button, justified text.** [Owner (2026-11), items 2 to 4.]
  - **Links icon.** The Links toggle on the Case Law Diary bar is always the chain-link icon, whatever Words or Logo is chosen for the header. Its label and title still name it.
  - **Home button.** My settings > Display > **Show the Home button** (`accounts.User.show_home_button`, migration `accounts/0027`, on by default) hides the header's Home button on every page. Alt+H (Option+H) still opens Home, and the Help page says so.
  - **Justified text.** A flag box (`inline-block`) or a Note's Edit button (`inline-flex`) after the text cannot break across lines. When it did not fit, it dropped to a line of its own, and the text's real last line was stretched to the full width as a middle line. With Justify text on, both now float to the right: the text's last line stays left-aligned, and the box sits at the right of that line, or just below it. `text-align-last: start` is also set. Without Justify text nothing changes.
- **D139. Investments header matches the other programs.** [Owner (2026-11): "In Investments, the header is very tall. Let it match the other programs."] `investments.css` still carried the mockup's own header rules (`padding-top: 1.25rem`, bottom-aligned rows, and the unused `a.nl`, `.ic` and `.menu-btn` rules). They overrode the shared shell and made the bar about 18px taller. They are removed, so `design.css` sets the header as for the Case Law Diary and the Fee Register. Found at the same time: the mockup's `--ox` colour was never defined in the suite, so the Alerts badge, primary and Delete buttons and alert banners lost their colour. It is now `--ox: var(--oxblood)`.
- **D140. Deleting from the Investments Catalogue.** [Owner (2026-11): "In Investment Catalogue, let me delete investments."] This replaces the plan 21.1 refusal ("only if no profile holds it").
  - **Preview first.** Settings > Catalogue > an investment > **Delete this investment** shows a preview with counts only, per profile: transactions, transfers, dividends, SIP schedules, folio numbers, dated values, and whether it is that profile's Default Index or a sectoral index. It also shows the shared stored, hand-entered and pinned prices.
  - **Confirming.** While any profile has anything about it, the Admin must type its name. A "Before changing the catalogue" backup is taken first; nothing is deleted if it fails.
  - **What is deleted.** For each profile, the records are written to that profile's History (as for the profile-level delete), then deleted in protected-link order. That covers transactions, transfers, dividends, SIP schedules, folios, dated values, guardrails, corporate action confirmations, saved alert states and its row. The instrument then goes with its prices, pins, symbols and corporate actions.
  - **Afterwards.** Default and sectoral index choices fall back by `SET_NULL`. Every affected profile is recomputed, and the catalogue history records the deletion.
  - **Refused.** Private investments, and Nifty 50, the built-in index (it would be re-created at once).
  - **Profile-level delete fixed.** The per-profile delete (Holdings > an investment > Delete) shares the same helper (`masters.delete_profile_records`). Before, it left dividends and SIP schedules behind, and as protected links they made the final removal fail with a server error.
- **D141. Investments follows the Fee Register's page layout.** [Owner (2026-11): "I do not need to see the page title on every page in Investments, as the page titles are already there in the Header / tab name. Remove them, the same way that you did in the Fee Register app. I want the UI design choices to be the same as in Fee Register."] This applies the Fee Register's "Page titles that repeat the header or the sub-tab" rule.
  - **Hidden titles.** A page that a header entry or a sub-tab opens (`pages.menu_slugs()`, for example Investments, Transactions, Performance and the Settings tabs) keeps its `<h1>` for screen readers only (`visually-hidden`). Every other page (forms, an investment, a broker, a report, Getting started, To complete, Import a statement) shows a plain `<h1>` in `.page-head`, the Fee Register's size, with no large serif title or double rule. The investment and catalogue item headings got the same size. Every page has exactly one `<h1>`, and a test checks this.
  - **What happened to the small grey line beside the title.** Where it said something the page does not, it became a hint: dividends are never part of Total Portfolio Value, history is kept permanently, the charts' rebasing date, and the Annual Report's "as at 31 March" or "to date". Counts and dates already shown on the page were dropped.
  - **Sub-tabs and buttons share one row.** As on the Fee Register's Memos page, the sub-tabs and the page's buttons sit in one `.tab-bar` row (block `page_actions` in `base_investments.html`), with the buttons on the right and the main one first. The buttons use the Fee Register's small button size. The sub-tabs look like its tabs: bold current tab with the accent underline. The headings within a page use the Fee Register's size.
  - **Transactions.** On Transactions, Add purchase, Add sale, Import a statement and Opening balance are buttons. Awaiting allotment, Transfer and Fund switch are under **More**, so the row fits on one line.
- **D142. Statement import adds missing investments.** [Owner (2026-11): "'Import a Statement' is a lost page, there is no button leading up to it" and yes to letting the import create missing investments after confirmation.]
  - **Links to the page.** Import a statement is now a button on Holdings > Investments and Holdings > Transactions. It is also mentioned in the Holdings empty state and in Getting started, whose stale "prices are typed by hand" line was corrected.
  - **The review lists missing investments.** A line whose investment the program does not have is no longer a dead end. The review lists each missing investment once under "Not in the program yet", with a guessed type, name, code, ISIN and currency, all from stored data only (no internet call):
    - **Funds:** found in the stored AMFI scheme list by ISIN, scheme code or name, so the AMFI code and full scheme name are filled in.
    - **Shares by ISIN:** the person types the code, because no ISIN-to-NSE-code table is stored.
    - **Bare symbols:** taken as NSE (`.NS`).
    - **Names:** a second name column in the file (for example Security Name beside ISIN) supplies the name when the investment column is a code.
  - **Adding them.** The person corrects and ticks them, then presses **Add … and check again**. `statement_import.create_missing` adds them all or none. A name and a code are required, because the code is how prices are fetched. If one is already in the program under another spelling (same code or name, `masters.find_similar`), that one is used instead of a duplicate.
  - **Matching the lines.** The file's text is then mapped to the investment (`aliases`, kept as `<token>-aliases.json` beside the staged file), so a corrected name still matches. The lines are reviewed again, and saving works as before: all or nothing, duplicates skipped, awaiting purchases completed. Prices arrive with the next Update Prices or the nightly fetch.
- **D143. Investments looks like the Fee Register.** [Owner (2026-11): "Match those too, as much as is feasible" (tables, filters, forms).]
  - **How.** A final block in `investments.css` copies the values of `ledger.css`, so the templates keep their own class names. `body.inv` no longer overrides `--tap`, `--hair` or `--panel`, so the ledger's control height (32px, 44px on touch), lines and cards apply.
  - **What now matches the Fee Register.**
    - **Tables** (`.tw` / `table.led`): the white card with a sticky header, 11px headings, 1rem cells and line separators.
    - **Filter rows and the tools row:** the `.filters` card.
    - **Form labels:** small capitals.
    - **Buttons** (`.btn`, `.p`, `.q`, `.sm`, `.dng`): button, primary, secondary, small and danger.
    - **Notices** (`.ban`, `.wa`, `.al`): notice, warning and error.
    - **Figures** (`.strip`): the Dashboard box grid.
    - **Hints, cards, tags and headings.**
  - **Two fixes.**
    - **Delete buttons.** In `ledger.css`, the rule that makes every submit button primary now skips `.dng`, so Delete buttons stay red. This has no effect on the Fee Register, which has no `.dng`.
    - **Sorting.** Sorting a table no longer strips the right-alignment classes of its headings (`shell.js`).
  - **Average cost.** The cost per unit is shown as a price, not as a money figure.
- **D144. The catalogue is open to everyone, and prices are on each investment's page.** [Owner (2026-11).]
  - **Who sees what.** Settings > Catalogue is visible to every member of Investments. Readers see it, Editors correct prices, and the Admin keeps:
    - shared details and symbols;
    - other profiles' names and their settings;
    - merge, price basis and delete;
    - adding to the catalogue.
  - **Shared block.** The price history, with the Editor's tools, is one block (`_price_history.html`). It is posted to `investments_price_action` and sits on both the catalogue page and the investment's own page:
    - correct or add a price;
    - delete a price, or put back a deleted one;
    - **Fetch the price history from** a chosen date to today. `catalogue.fetch_from` asks the source first and outside any transaction, so nothing stored changes if it fails or sends nothing. Fetched prices in the range are replaced; hand-entered and uploaded prices are kept.
    - **Remove older prices.** `trim_before_first_purchase` removes the fetched prices from before the first purchase. Prices are shared, so every profile's records count. The price that values the first purchase is kept, and so are uploads, hand-entered prices and pins. It is refused for indices. A preview comes first, and the prices can be fetched back later.
  - **Privacy.** The catalogue page shows no date from any profile's records (plan 21.1). The first purchase date and the trim tool are therefore on the investment's own page, which shows the profile's own first purchase. If another profile bought earlier, the page says so without giving that profile's date.
- **D145. Current PE removed.** The `Instrument.latest_pe` field is gone, along with its form field, its catalogue entry and its line on the investment page (migration `investments/0013`). The next results date stays.
- **D146. History names the portfolio.** The History table's Who column became **Portfolio** (`history.attach_portfolio_names`).
  - **Transaction, dividend and folio:** their own portfolio.
  - **Transfer:** "From > To".
  - **Switch:** its legs' portfolio.
  - **Alternative values and confirmations:** blank.
- **D147. Short figures are the Fee Register's.** [Owner (2026-11): "Money display, for k, lac, cr - let these options be the same as is shown in Fee Register."]
  - **The rule.** Short rupee figures now follow `fees.services.money.format_money` exactly:
    - below 10, whole rupees;
    - below 1 lakh, `k`;
    - below 1 crore, `lac`;
    - otherwise `cr`.

    Up to 2 decimals are shown, with trailing zeros dropped, and a space only before a suffix longer than one letter: ₹85.4k, ₹24.13 lac, ₹2.41 cr.
  - **Labels.** The options now use the Fee Register's names: the tools toggle reads **Short / Full**, and the Display setting offers "Short (Thousand, Lac, Crore)" or "Full Figures". The stored value is still `auto`.
  - **Unchanged.** Full figures, foreign currencies and the sign style are as before.
  - **Tests.** The golden table follows the new rule, and a test compares the result with the Fee Register's function. The JavaScript mirror changed with it.
  - **Fee Register bug found.** The Fee Register's own "Suffix" settings are stored but never applied, because its template filter does not pass them.
- **D148. CAGR is compared with the index's CAGR.** With the CAGR measure, the Performance page and its export compare each investment's CAGR with the index's CAGR between the same two dates: the investment's first and last stored price in the period. The sectoral index is compared the same way, and Alpha is the difference. Before, the index column showed an index XIRR.

- **D149. The Overview's figures can be chosen.** [Owner (2026-11): "Allow me to choose the metrics shown in the Overview. Suggest others to me. I want to see various things for the entire history, 5 years, 3 years and 1 year."]
  - **Where it is chosen.** Overview > Customise is for Editors, and the choice is per profile (`InvestmentSettings.overview`, `services/overview.py`).
  - **The boxes at the top:** current value, invested, gain, portfolio XIRR, index XIRR, Alpha, dividends received and the number of holdings. The first six are the standard choice.
  - **The "Over time" table** shows each chosen figure for the chart's periods: 1M, 3M, 6M, 1Y, 3Y, 5Y and All (the same day counts as the chart). Any period can be left out. Choices stored before this (`5`, `3`, `1`) are read as 5Y, 3Y and 1Y.
  - **Figures in the table:**
    - your XIRR, the index XIRR and Alpha;
    - the time-weighted return (yearly) and the index's change;
    - the value at the start and the value at the end;
    - money put in, money taken out and fresh investments;
    - gain in the period;
    - dividends;
    - gains booked on sales, first in first out;
    - largest fall (drawdown).
  - **Standard table:** XIRR, index XIRR, Alpha, fresh investments and gain, for all seven periods.
  - **Breakdown table.** Below the chart, "Portfolio value" by Type, by Portfolio or by Broker (`?breakdown=type|portfolio|broker`, default Type; plain links like the chart periods). Columns: Invested, Value, Gain, Gain %, Share, with a total row equal to the Overview value. All three are worked out with the cached Overview figures; a holding's value is shared over its lots by units, and a purchase awaiting allotment is its own group.
  - **How a period is worked out.** It uses the Overview's portfolio, broker and as-at date. The value at its start counts as money put in on that day, as on the Performance page.
  - **Shorter histories.** A period longer than the history is shown as the whole history and marked *.
  - **Approximate figures.** The time-weighted return and the largest fall come from the chart's samples (at most 400 over the whole history), so they are approximate for short periods.
  - **Cost and caching.** Only the chosen figures and periods are worked out. The table is cached with the rest of the Overview. Bench figures are in `docs/INVESTMENTS_RAM.md`; the bench's `dashboard` operation now times the whole Overview.
- **D150. Indices.**
  - **Settings > Indices** lists every index with what uses it.
    - An Editor adds one by its Yahoo symbol. Yahoo is asked for the whole history first (see D165), so an unknown symbol is refused and nothing is made.
    - Ten NSE sector indices are offered as one-click suggestions. Each is checked with Yahoo when added.
    - An added index can be deleted; Nifty 50, the built-in one, cannot (D153). If an index is in use, its name must be typed, and the choices fall back: the Default Index goes back to Nifty 50, and a sector index to none. This is logged in the catalogue history.
  - **Sector index of each investment.** It is chosen from a list on the investment's page (Your details) or for all investments at once on Settings > Indices. The Admin's catalogue form became a list too, instead of typing an id.
  - **Pins.** A chosen sector index is pinned at once on the dates of that investment's transactions, from stored prices. The nightly pin step now also pins each transaction's sector index while sector comparisons are on.
  - **Prices.** Only indices in use are fetched nightly, as before. An index chosen for the first time gets its whole history that night.
  - **Buttons.** In `ledger.css`, the rule that makes submit buttons primary now skips `.q` (Investments' secondary buttons), so a secondary button that submits a form stays secondary.
- **D151. Column picker in Investments.** Settings > Display > **Column picker**, per profile and off by default, puts the Fee Register's Columns menu (`static/js/fees/columns.js`) above every Investments table with three or more columns. `columns-on.js` marks the tables. The first column always stays, and choices are remembered in the browser, table by table, as in the Fee Register. The key includes the number of columns, so a table whose columns depend on a setting keeps a choice per layout.
- **D152. Annual Report: "Fresh investments".** Line 4, formerly "Amount re-invested", is now **Fresh investments**: amount invested less amount sold in the year. It is negative when more was taken out than put in. The page and the export both use the new name.
- **D153. Nifty 50 is the only built-in index.** [Owner (2026-11).] `masters.BUILTIN_INDICES` now holds only Nifty 50.
  - **Migration `investments/0016`** removes the other seven seeded by 0002 (Sensex, Nifty Next 50, Nifty Bank, Nifty Midcap 150, Nifty Smallcap 250, Nifty IT and S&P 500), with their prices and pins.
  - **Indices in use stay.** An index that a profile uses as its Default Index or as a sector index is kept, as an ordinary added index that can be deleted.
  - **Re-adding.** Settings > Indices offers the removed NSE and BSE ones as one-click suggestions, beside the sector indices. S&P 500 is not in the list; add it by its symbol, `^GSPC`, with currency USD.
- **D154. Decimals shown.** Settings > Display has two choices, per profile and for display only. Calculations, stored values and Excel exports stay exact.
  - **Decimals in units:** standard (as many as needed, up to 4) or exactly 0 to 4.
  - **Decimals in full rupee figures:** standard (paise only when there are some) or exactly 0 to 2. [Owner: "should only be for figures in long mode - in short mode, it will always be up to 2 decimal places".] Short figures (k, lac, cr) always show up to 2 decimals.
  - **How it works.** `viewutil.page` puts the profile's choice in a context variable read by the `units`, `inr` and `inr_signed` filters. The Python formatter and its JavaScript mirror take the same `decimals` option, which applies to full figures only; the golden table has cases for it.
  - **Not affected.** Prices per unit (`price`) and percentages are unchanged.
- **D155. Importing existing holdings from a sheet.** [Owner (2026-11): an Excel sheet with date, name, code, type, folio, portfolio, broker, buy or sell, price and quantity.] Import a statement now reads such a sheet. The columns are found by their headings, in any order, and **Download a sample sheet** gives the headings with two example rows.
  - **Investment.** A code (NSE code, AMFI scheme code or ISIN) that matches an investment uses it; failing that, a matching name is used. Any other investment is **added on saving** from the row's name, code and **Type**, with no separate step.
    - **Type words:** Share, Equity, Stock or ETF means a share; Mutual Fund, MF or Fund means a fund; Foreign means a foreign share; Alternative, PPF, FD, Bond, Gold and the like mean an alternative investment, which needs no code.
    - **Codes:** a bare share code gets `.NS`.
    - **Funds:** a fund found in the stored AMFI list takes its AMFI name and code.
    - **Duplicates:** an investment already in the program under another spelling (same code or name) is used instead of a new one.
    - **Corrections:** the review lists every investment to be added, and they can be corrected first with "Add and check again", as before.
  - **Portfolio and Broker on each row.** They are matched by name, ignoring case. One the profile does not have is added on saving, and the review says so. The page's portfolio and broker are now optional and are used only for rows that leave those cells blank. A row with neither is a problem to fix.
  - **Headings.** "Transaction type" is buy or sell, and plain "Type" is the investment's type. Portfolio and Broker need an exact heading, so "Brokerage" stays charges.
  - **Saving.** Everything is saved together: the new investments, portfolios, brokers and folios, then the transactions in date order. Rows already recorded are skipped, so the same sheet can be loaded twice safely.
- **D156. Clear a profile's transaction history.** [Owner (2026-11): "allow me to clear the existing transaction history for a profile".] Settings > General > **Clear transaction history** (Admin only) shows what would be deleted, with counts, and asks for the profile's name to be typed.
  - **Deleted:** purchases and sales (awaiting ones too), transfers, fund switches, dividends, SIP schedules, folio numbers, alternative investments' dated values, corporate action confirmations, saved alert states, and the cached figures.
  - **History log:** kept unless the box "Also delete the History log" is ticked.
  - **Kept:** the investments in the profile's list (with notes, links and sector index), portfolios, brokers, guardrails and settings, and everything shared.
  - **Safety:** a "Before clearing profile" backup is taken first; nothing is deleted if it fails. Other profiles are never touched.
  - **Use:** to start again with a corrected holdings sheet (D155).
- **D157. AMFI's file is read from the end of each line.** [Owner (2026-11): the nightly job said "AMFI's file had none of the schemes asked for".] AMFI's `NAVAll.txt` now puts the plan and option in columns of their own after the scheme name (8 fields, not 6), so the NAV and date are the **last two** fields and the name is everything between the ISINs and them, joined with " - ". Old 6-field lines read as before. The same change is in `tools/price_helper.py`.
- **D158. Bhavcopy as a price source.** [Owner (2026-11): "Build in bhavcopy and a google sheets system."] `adapters/bhavcopy.py` reads NSE's UDiFF equity bhavcopy (zip), NSE's `ind_close_all` index closes and BSE's UDiFF equity bhavcopy, line by line, keeping only the rows asked for; the addresses are settings (`INVESTMENTS_BHAV_NSE_URL`, `INVESTMENTS_BHAV_NSE_INDEX_URL`, `INVESTMENTS_BHAV_BSE_URL`). `services/bhav.py` decides when it is used, chosen on Price sources (stored in `SourceSetting` under `bhavcopy`):
  - **Off** (the default, so nothing changes until the owner switches it on);
  - **After Yahoo:** fill what Yahoo missed;
  - **Before Yahoo:** Yahoo is asked only for what the files did not cover.

  The files are raw closes, which equal split-adjusted closes for the newest days, so they fill only the last 14 days of an investment that already has a price history (never one with none, or Yahoo would afterwards fetch only from that day). Stored rows are never replaced except a Google Sheet row (source `helper`), which gives way to the exchange's official close. Matching: `X.NS` is NSE symbol X, `123456.BO` is BSE scrip code 123456, the ISIN is a second key, an index goes by its NSE name (built-in map, else the investment's name), and a `bhavcopy` symbol on the catalogue page replaces the code. A 404 is "no file that day" (holidays); a web page instead of the file, or no file for four weekdays, is reported. Sensex is not in NSE's file. Downloads are cached for half an hour per process for the Update Prices batches.
- **D159. The Google Sheet.** It uses the helper's token and endpoints, so it needs `PRICE_HELPER_ENABLED`.
  - **The list.** `GET /investments/api/prices/sheet/` gives the tracked shares, indices and foreign investments (codes, names, suggested GOOGLEFINANCE tickers: `NSE:X`, `BOM:123456`, a built-in index map; a `google` symbol on the catalogue page replaces it). It never sends holdings. Funds are left out.
  - **The script.** `tools/google_sheet_prices.gs` (shown ready to copy, with the site address filled in, on Price sources > Set up the Google Sheet) writes the list, looks prices up, and posts them to the ingest endpoint with `fill_only` and `via: "sheet"`. It runs on timers at 7 pm and 11 pm India time.
  - **Storing.** A sheet price is stored only for a day with no price, as source `helper`, logged as the `google_sheet` run. Yahoo's next fetch and bhavcopy replace it.
- **D160. Bonus shares, split units and demergers as transactions; per-case choices.** [Owner (2026-11): bonus and split shares, and shares in a demerged company, should be importable at no cost and marked as such; a demerger should adjust the cost of the parent and the new shares for the calculations only; the original transaction is never affected; each decision is taken case by case.] Builds on D125; it replaces the old advice to correct a demerger with the cost override, which edits the original row.
  - **How units were acquired.** A "buy" row has an `origin`: purchase (the default; every existing row), bonus, split, demerger (shares received on a demerger) or other (a gift or transfer in). The first four after purchase may name their `corporate_action`. A purchase still needs a price above 0. Bonus, split and demerger rows are saved with price 0 (a bonus is simply a lot at no cost dated its allotment or ex-date); "other" may carry a price. Only a "buy" can have an origin other than purchase; an opening balance, an awaiting entry and a sale cannot.
  - **Never counted twice.** A bonus or split is added either by the program (the response's `method` is `events`, the old behaviour and the default) or by transactions with that origin (`method` is `transactions`). Applying it one way while units exist the other way is refused, a transaction linked to an action the program adds itself is refused, and the statement import skips such a row. `applied(for_units=False)` still lists every applied split and bonus because a restated price series needs them all (D125, plan 16.2).
  - **Decided case by case.** Each profile answers, for each action, when it applies it: for a bonus or split, who adds the units (the program, "I record the units as transactions", or "create the transactions for me", which makes one editable zero-cost entry for every portfolio, broker and folio that held units the day before the ex-date, dated the ex-date, whole units for a share, never rounded up); for a demerger, whether the new shares keep the purchase dates of the parent lots they came from (`inherit_dates`, default no; a tax judgement for the CA). Splits stay automatic by default: a zero-cost lot for the new units gives the right total cost but the wrong cost on a part sale and a new holding period, so choosing transactions for a split is allowed but the page says so. A detected "split" can be changed to the bonus it really was (a price source reports an Indian bonus issue as a split) while nobody has applied it.
  - **Demerger.** `CorporateAction.kind = demerger` names the new company (`target_instrument`) and the percent of the parent's cost that moves to it (`cost_percent_to_target`, 0 to 100, so 0 leaves all the cost with the parent; from the company's notice, for the CA to confirm). Once applied, on the ex-date every parent lot loses that share of its cost (`lots.DEMERGER_OUT`; units, dates and original transactions unchanged). The new shares are recorded as purchases with origin demerger (the units actually received, so a fraction or cash in lieu is handled by the statement); each is made of pieces that take their units and cost from the parent lots held in the same portfolio and broker the day before the ex-date, by units (`lots.DEMERGER_IN`, `corporate.receipt_sources`). When several receipts share a portfolio and broker, each moves its part of the cost by the units it received, so the cost moved equals the cost the parent lost. The parent's lots are replayed as they stood then, so earlier demergers carry through. The Corporate actions page warns when cost has left the parent but no shares are recorded for a portfolio and broker, because that cost would otherwise vanish.
  - **Original and adjusted cost.** Each lot and each sale match remembers how much of its cost came from a demerger (`adjustment`); `original_cost` is the cost the original transactions give. The investment page and the Annual Report show a "Cost adjusted" tag with the original cost, and the Annual Report export notes it. Gains use the adjusted cost. XIRR keeps using the real cash flows (a bonus or demerger receipt has none), so a parent and its demerged shares each look odd on their own and right together.
  - **Statement import.** An Origin column (exact headings only), or a word such as "Bonus" in the Transaction type cell, sets the origin; the review page has a "Treated as" choice on every buy line, kept until the file is saved. A row with units but no price and no origin is a problem, never a purchase. A bonus is linked to a recorded bonus within five days of its date, or saved unlinked; a split or a demerger needs its recorded (and, for a demerger, applied) action first. A row already added by the program, or matching units already recorded against the same action, is skipped as covered or a duplicate.
- **D161. "Held only" tick box.** Held means `Holding.is_held` (units held, or a purchase awaiting allotment); `holdings.held_instrument_ids` returns those ids. Report pages send a hidden `hf=1` marker with the tick box `held=1`, so an unticked box (marker, no `held`) is off and a page opened without the marker uses its default (`viewutil.held_only`). On by default: Performance (the All row then covers the rows shown), Charts (the investment list; anything already picked stays listed; the chart cache key does not change because the data does not), and the Other reports best and worst and index scoreboard. The dividend report and the data check show the box too and, since the owner's round 18 request, start ticked like every other (the dividend report then says its total covers held investments only; untick to see everything). Not shown where it would do nothing: Total Portfolio Value and the Annual Report holdings (both already list only what is held; realised gains always include sold investments), and the reports that total by year or group. On the Buy, Sell, awaiting, opening balance, transfer, switch (fund sold) and dividend forms the tick box filters the Investment drop-down in the browser (`data-held` on each option, `held-only.js`, kept apart from `txn-form.js` so the dividend, transfer and switch forms do not get its broker and folio behaviour); the chosen option is never hidden, the server accepts every investment, and without JavaScript the box stays hidden and all investments are listed. Starts ticked on every form (Buy, Sell, awaiting, Opening balance, Transfer, Switch, Dividend); an edit starts unticked when the entry is for something no longer held, so the chosen investment still shows. **Sell form streamlined:** the date, investment, portfolio, broker, folio, units and price show; Charges and Note sit under a "Charges and note" fold (`more_fields` on the form, rendered by `_form.html`) that opens when either holds a value or an error. The posted fields are unchanged.
- **D162. Fee Register: Statement mirrors Ledger, Ledger totals, Group page.** [Owner (2026-11).] The Statement (screen and Excel, for a firm, a group or a matter) now has exactly the Ledger columns (Date, Particulars, Matter, Memo, Payment, TDS, Balance); both share `templates/fees/_ledger_table.html`. The Ledger and the Statement both show a `Totals (n)` row under Memo, Payment and TDS (`ledger.ledger_totals`), never under Balance, on screen and in the Excel export. The memo page no longer has the "Statement of the matter (Excel)" button (the route `fees_memo_statement` remains). On a firm page each Group is a pill (`.group-pill`) linking to `fees_group_detail` (`/fees/firms/<id>/groups/<gid>/`), which has Statement, Ledger, View Outstanding, Edit Group (rename, Editor), New Matter (firm and group preselected) and the group's matters with + Memo. A group's Statement, Ledger and exports reuse the firm URLs with `?group=<gid>` (a group of another firm is a 404); the title is `Statement - Firm - Group`. Rename moved from the firm page to the group page.

- **Keep my place in every program (settings redesign, FEAT-003).** [Owner: "Whenever a page refreshes, keep the spot where I was ... set up in Case Law Diary, not sure if it has been replicated in the others yet."] `static/js/keep-place.js` is loaded once by `templates/base_suite.html`, which the Diary, Home, Fee Register and Investments shells (and so settings and admin pages) all extend, so it already covered every program for reloads, Back/Forward and POST forms that return to the same address. **Added:** a GET form (filters, period pickers) that submits to the same page now carries the place to the new address, since the query string makes it a different key. Forms that go to another page still open at the top. Pages without `n-` entries use the plain scroll height, and the script re-applies it after `load` for late layout. Test: `diary/tests/test_hyperlinks.py` (`KeepPlaceEverywhereTests`).

- **Investments tools bar slimmed (settings redesign, FEAT-004).** [Owner: the Portfolio, Broker, As at, Update Price and Short / Full boxes need not be on every page.] The selectors row (Portfolio, Broker, As at, plus the price status line) now shows only on pages that use the selection (Overview, Holdings, Reports; pages rendered with `selectors=False` have no row). Short / Full is chosen in Settings > Display ("Number display for you"); a hidden `.toggle[data-post]` element in the header keeps `shell.js` working and `/investments/prefs/money/` stays. Update Prices is a small refresh button in the header (`#update-prices`, progress in `#update-status-hdr`, and in `#update-status` where the row is shown). The Holdings Type and Status filters stay on one line (`form.filters`, no wrap). The Alerts entry in the header shows a plain red dot (`aria-label="Alerts need attention"`) instead of the count; `alert-badge.js` still fills it after load when the alerts were not yet worked out.

- **Header cleanup (settings redesign, FEAT-005).** [Owner: "Remove settings from the headers, as clicking on the user-name logo anyway takes you to settings. Further, remove the option of Logos and Words in the header. Stick with words, except for the few forced logos (username, search, home, log out). Remove the option to remove the home logo."] The Case Law Diary, Fee Register and Investments headers no longer have a Settings link (the user badge opens My settings, whose tabs lead to each program's settings; the settings pages themselves stay). The Words or Logo choice is gone: `accounts.suite` has no brand modes (the `SuiteName` rows `brand_*` are ignored), the Appearance form has no header bar fields, `header_words` and `home_header_words` are always True, and `User.header_style` is removed. `User.show_home_button` is removed too, so Home is always shown (Alt+H unchanged). Migration `accounts/0033_remove_user_header_style_and_show_home_button`. Restoring an older backup still works: the restore ignores fields that no longer exist. This supersedes D84 and the Words or Logo item (3.1) above.

- **Settings redesigned as one tab strip (settings redesign, FEAT-006).** [Owner: "re-do settings entirely ... Users & Profiles (Users, Profiles), Account and Profile View, Backup, Display (and a new Start Screens page), and one page for each sub-program. Rationalise and make consistent the formatting and header systems. No settings left out, no duplication, not too complicated. Tell me about redundant settings."] The `Admins` and `Settings` header items on Home became one `Settings` item opening `my_settings`; its tab strip (`accounts.context_processors.SETTINGS_TABS`, `templates/_settings_tabs.html`) is the same on every settings page, including inside the diary, Fee Register and Investments (the programs' own Settings pages include it through `crumb_nav`). Tabs: Users & Profiles (second row Users, Profiles, Profile Access), Account and Profile View, Backup, Display (second row My Display, Names), Start Screens, then one tab per program the user can open; admin tabs are hidden from others. Every settings page uses the same layout: a visually hidden `h1` named like its tab, a one-line intro, left-hand sections (`settings-sections.js`), one Save per form, and a "Only you / Everyone in this profile / Everyone" tag on each setting. Personal program settings are shown once, on the program's page, and saved through `my_settings` with a `tab` (`diary`, `fees`, `investments`): Case Law Diary > My Number Formats (number formats, date in Word file names, Remove citation hyphen), Fee Register > Display > My items per page, Investments > Display > Investments period. Start Screens holds the Open-after-login program, the Case Law Diary opening page and Subject, the Fee Register Opening page, and the new `User.investments_start_page` (migration 0034, read by `investments.views.landing`). The Account page replaces the old Account section with the email, the header badge letters and the password, and adds Profile View (a list of the user's profiles per program with Use this profile, which is `profile_switch`; no stored setting). `MyAccountForm`, `MyDisplayForm`, `MyStartForm`, `MyCitationForm` and `MyInvestmentsForm` each own their fields, because an unticked box in a posted form means off, so a field is saved only by the form that shows it. Old addresses: `?tab=diary|numbers|fees|investments` on `my_settings` redirect to the program's page; `/investments/settings/catalogue|price-*` answer 308. The Investments **Catalogue** (the shared catalogue, Price history, Price sources) is a top-level Investments page of its own (`catalogue/`), no longer in Settings. The duplicated "Starting number display" on Investments General was removed (it stays on Display).

- **D163. Redundant settings removed (settings clean-up).** [Owner: "Go with your suggestions for the redundant settings and options."] **Removed:** `diary.UserSettings.export_custom_name` and `time_zone` (exports use India time; diary 0024); `Profile.reading_all_default` (accounts 0035, profiles on "remember" with the box unticked became `always_off`, the rest stay on "remember"; the "remember" choice stays and a browser with no remembered view gets the whole Subject; the JSON backup converts old files); `FeesSettings.default_additional_details_left` and `right` (fees 0007; the Excel backup still writes the two desktop columns, empty, and restore ignores them); `InvestmentSettings.hidden_pages` and the Investments Pages tab (investments 0021); `landing_program` and `colour_theme` on the Admin user form (the fields stay, set on Start Screens and Display). **Fee Register (fees 0008):** `FeesSettings.default_tab` and `items_per_page` are gone; the Opening page and Items per page are personal only (`FeesUserPrefs`, key `settings`), standard Memos and 50, and the migration copied each profile's non-standard value to members with no value of their own. `statistics_enabled`, `dashboard_enabled` and `dashboard_kpis_enabled` are one switch, `statistics_enabled` ("Statistics and Dashboard" in Settings > Years and statistics); off hides the Statistics and Dashboard pages and the Dashboard figures; it stays on only if all three were on. Excel backups: the desktop `Settings.default_tab` column is still written (at `memos`) and ignored on restore; `WebSettings` no longer writes `dashboard_enabled`, `dashboard_kpis_enabled` or `items_per_page`, and an older file carrying them restores (any Dashboard switch off turns Statistics off; `items_per_page` is ignored). **Naming:** the Home Page Name (`display_name`) and Header Name (`header_name`) are edited only on Users & Profiles > Profiles; Case Law Diary > Settings > Reading no longer has them. No model change; both fields and `User.header_initials` stay. **Kept:** `show_status_dots` (legend) and `hide_payment_when_paid` (Payment button) do different things. The list is in `docs/SETTINGS.md` section 9.

- **Toast messages (toasts round).** [Owner: messages pushed the page down.] Django messages are now a fixed stack at the top centre (`ul.messages.toasts` in `templates/base_suite.html`, styled in `site.css`), so page content never moves. Before this, messages sat inside `<main>` above the page content and pushed it down. `static/js/toasts.js` adds a Dismiss button to each, auto-dismisses success/info/debug after about 5 seconds (paused on hover or focus, no fade under reduced motion) and keeps error and warning until dismissed; without JS they stay visible. Success/info use `role=status`, error/warning `role=alert`. **Alternative placements** are only a class on the `ul` (replace nothing else): `toasts--top-right`, `toasts--bottom-right`, `toasts--bottom-center`, and `toasts--banner` (the old inline banner, which does shift the layout). Default is top centre.

- **Buy and sell pages in fewer lines, several transactions at once.** The Buy form shows Date, Investment, Portfolio, Broker, Units and Price; Allotment date, Charges, Folio, New folio, How acquired, Corporate action and Note sit under one fold (`more_fields`, opens when one holds a value or error); awaiting forms fold Charges and Note. Help text: Date is the trade (paid) date; Allotment date is when units were credited, if later: it becomes `bought_on` (holding period, FIFO order) while Date stays `paid_on`. "Add another transaction" (`txn-rows.js`, JS only) clones a full card from a `<template>` into a rounded `.txn-card`: every card has the same fields as the first (Date, Investment, Portfolio, Broker, Units, Price and the fold), its own Held only box, and posts as `x1-date`, `x1-instrument`, ... with the card numbers in `extra_rows`, each bound as a `TransactionForm` with that prefix (at most 30 extra). `txn-form.js` and `held-only.js` wire each card (and any card added later, announced by the `txn-card-added` event). `txn_form` validates every card, then saves them in date order inside `db.transaction.atomic()`, so a refused card (form error or service error, shown on that card) saves nothing, and several sales of one holding are checked one after another by `add_transaction` against what is held on each date (later entries included). Sell cards end with "Units available to sell", read live from `investments_available_units` (`/investments/transactions/available/`) with `instrument`, `date`, `portfolio`, `broker` and `folio` (blank for none): the units in that one place as at the date. Edit pages show one transaction only; the posted fields of the first transaction are unchanged.
- **Excel exports of transactions and holdings (toasts and exports round).** [Owner: export the transaction history, per investment and overall, and all holdings.] `?export=xlsx` on `/investments/transactions/` (Transactions, Transfers and Dividends sheets, every row, same portfolio / broker / as-at / search / buy-sell filters, no paging), on each investment page (`<Investment name> transactions.xlsx`, name stripped to letters, digits, spaces, dots, dashes and underscores) and on the Holdings list (the rows left after the Type and Status filters, exact figures). Writers are in `investments/exports.py` (`transaction_sheets`, `holdings_sheet`, `safe_file_name`); buttons are "Export to Excel" in the page actions (Records heading on the investment page). Other exports: Dividends, Investment catalogue, Performance and the other reports, Charts, Total Portfolio Value, Annual (all `?export=xlsx`); Fee Register Excel/Word downloads; Case Law Diary Word export; backup files; price archive zip.

## Round: investment page, one chart at a time, Refresh price
- An investment's page reads General (figures, where it is held, details, one chart), then Purchase by purchase, then Records, then price entry, folios, details and the rest. Existing anchors (`#price`, `#value-chart`, `#prices`) are unchanged.
- Only one chart is drawn: `?chart=price` (default) or `?chart=value`, a plain link switch with no script, kept by the period, `?on=` and `?div=` links. Only the chosen chart is worked out. An alternative investment has no price feed, so it shows the value chart alone.
- The price history carries a dashed index line ("Same start in <index>"): the investment's sectoral index when set, else the default index, scaled to equal the price on the first priced day of the window. Without index prices the line is simply left out.
- Refresh price posts the one investment to the same Update Prices endpoint (`instrument`), limited to what `fetch.tracked_instruments` allows for the profile. It skips the one-minute pause of the whole-list button; the one-at-a-time lock still applies. Edit (admin only, shared investments) links to the Catalogue page.
- Purchase by purchase, Sold portions and Records show a Portfolio column (a lot's bucket is portfolio, broker, folio).

## Round: price window
- **Prices are collected only while an investment is in play.** `fetch.fetch_window` works out, per investment and across ALL profiles, a start (the earliest transaction, opening balance or transfer date, awaiting allotments included) and an end (none while units, an awaiting purchase, a SIP to confirm or a future-dated row remain in any profile, else the last row's date plus 7 days so the sale-date valuation has a price). Units are replayed by `holdings.compute`, so bonus, split and demerger units and applied events count. It is computed on demand, nothing is stored or cached.
- `fetch.tracked_instruments` leaves out an investment nobody has bought and one sold out more than 7 days ago; the default index, sectoral indices and ^NSEI are always tracked, from the earliest purchase of any profile. The first fetch starts 7 days before the window opens (a purchase on a non-trading day is valued at the previous close); later fetches go from the last stored date but never before the window start. Fetched rows, splits and dividends outside the window are dropped before storing (Yahoo, mfapi.in, AMFI and bhavcopy). Sold-out investments are no longer counted as "not updated".
- Prices already stored outside a window are left alone. A split re-fetch (`refetch_full`) still replaces the instrument's fetched rows, but asks only from the window start. An investment with no transactions has no feed until the first purchase is recorded (the next run fetches it).

## Round: compact rows (controls and status share a line)
- Rule: controls that belong together sit on one flex-wrap row; they only drop to a new line when the width runs out. Do not give a short item `flex-basis:100%`.
- Investments: the update status line shares the Portfolio / Broker / As at row (status to the right, wrapping on narrow screens). The Columns menu joins the buttons of the section heading above its table (Customise and Columns on one line on Overview) instead of a row of its own; tables without a `.sec` heading keep the right-aligned row. Section and button-row margins are smaller.

## Corporate actions logged automatically or approved (per kind)
- Decision: `InvestmentSettings.corporate_auto` is one JSON dict (keys dividend, split, bonus, demerger, other) rather than five columns; a missing key means "Ask me to approve", so nothing changes until a kind is switched on (investments 0022).
- Automatic application reuses `corporate.respond` (so the history, the sale checks and the cache bump are the same as a click) and runs from `corporate.record`, from a profile's purchase, and when the setting is saved. Anything it refuses stays pending.
- Dividends: `DividendSignal.logged_for` lists the profiles already logged so a deleted automatic dividend is not recreated. Signals older than 60 days are not back-filled.

## Demerger allotments at cost 0, and split or bonus units on Transactions
- Demerger: shares of the new company are recorded as a purchase with origin "Received on a demerger". The price is always stored as 0 (`ZERO_PRICE_ORIGINS`); their cost is taken from the parent's lots by the action's cost percentage, so nothing is entered by hand and nothing is counted twice. The Corporate actions page has an "Add allotted shares" link (row and warning banner) that opens the Add purchase form with `?action=<id>`, prefilled with the origin, the demerger, the new company and the ex-date.
- Split and bonus: units added by the program (method `events`) are NOT turned into transactions, because that would double count. `corporate.automatic_unit_rows` derives one read-only row per portfolio, broker and folio from the units held the day before the ex-date; the Transactions page merges them into the page by date (a row belongs to the page whose date range holds it, so pagination never repeats or loses one), labels them "Split (added automatically)" or "Bonus shares (added automatically)", shows price 0 and no Edit button, and leaves them out of `total` and of every amount. The Excel export adds an "Added automatically" sheet. Real transactions of origin split, bonus or demerger carry their origin as a tag. The Buy / Sell filter "Sales" hides the derived rows.

## Round: Taxed as Interest
- **Taxed as Interest.** `Instrument.taxed_as_interest` (shared, default off; set when adding an investment or by an Admin under Shared details) marks an investment whose sale profit or loss is Interest Income, not Capital Gains (a record-keeping aid; the CA decides). `Holding.interest_income` carries it per investment (and `Holding.realised` then excludes it); `annual.Report.interest_income` carries it per year. The Annual Report shows an Interest income line, a Term of "Interest income" in Sale by sale, and per-portfolio figures. Its Excel export has an Interest income row in Summary, its own "Interest income" sheet (so "Capital gains" lists capital gains only), and the column in By portfolio. The Total income summary report has an Interest income column, added into its Total. The Holdings export has an Interest income column next to Realised gain. The investment page shows Interest income beside Realised gain. XIRR, value and unrealised figures are unchanged.

- **Demerger shares are entered for you, and actions can be edited.** Applying a demerger calls `corporate.sync_demerger_allotments`: one zero-price entry (origin demerger, `Transaction.auto_created = True`) for each portfolio, broker and folio that held the parent the day before the ex-date, dated the ex-date, units = held x new / old rounded down (whole shares; four decimals for a fund). Running it again keeps entries that still match and replaces those that do not, so nothing is counted twice; marking the action not applicable removes them. Editing a transaction (`edit_transaction`) clears `auto_created`, so an entry the person edited, or recorded themselves, is never replaced and its portfolio, broker and folio get no automatic entry. `corporate.update` (page `corporate-actions/<id>/edit/`) changes the date, ratio, notes and, for a demerger, the new company and cost percentage, after it was applied too; lots are derived from the history each time, so they follow. It is refused when another profile has answered the action or recorded units against it, when the new company is changed while the person's own entries exist, and for the date or ratio of a split or bonus whose earlier prices were already restated. `inherit_dates` now defaults to off.

- **Override the units allotted on a demerger.** `CorporateActionResponse.units_override` (JSON, investments 0025) maps `portfolio:broker:folio` (folio 0 for none) to the units the person was really allotted, per profile. `corporate.sync_demerger_allotments` uses it in place of the ratio figure (whole units for a share, four decimals for a fund, 0 means no entry; an override for a bucket that held nothing the day before the ex-date is ignored but kept), so kept/replaced logic and `auto_created` tagging are unchanged. `corporate.set_units_overrides` validates and saves (blank clears) and makes the entries again; `update` and `respond` never recompute it, so it survives editing the ratio and applying again. The cost split is untouched. The edit page of an applied demerger shows the table `demerger-allotments` (Portfolio, Broker, Folio, Units held, Calculated units, Override units); a bucket where the person recorded shares themselves shows "recorded by you" and has no input.

- **D164. Data source formats are Admin-editable data.** [Owner (2026-11): changed external files must be readable again without code edits.] `services/source_formats.py` holds `DEFAULTS`, one entry per source with exactly the built-in behaviour (address template, delimiter, header row, per-field column alias lists, date formats, NSE series kept, JSON paths), and `get(key)` lays the stored changes (`DataSourceFormat`, investments 0027, only the differing keys) over them. It is declarative only: addresses are filled by a placeholder whitelist (never `str.format` on arbitrary attributes, never `eval`), and `validate` checks every value against the type of its built-in value (unknown keys, wrong types, long text, non-http(s) addresses, unlisted placeholders, bad date formats and paths are refused); a stored value that no longer validates is ignored in favour of the built-in format. Headers are matched tolerantly (case, spacing, underscores, BOM; the first alias in the list wins), so the defaults read what the adapters always read. The parsing functions of the adapters (`adapters/bhavcopy.py`, `amfi.py`, `mfapi.py`, `nse_list.py`, `yahoo.py`) take the format as an optional argument and default to `get(...)`, which falls back to the built-in format when there is no database, so the existing adapter tests are unchanged. The imports read their alias lists from the same place (`price_import.header_columns`, `statement_import.guess_columns`).
  - **No silent break.** A file whose required columns are missing, or from which no row can be read although it was not empty, raises `FormatError` (a `FetchError` that names the source key, the missing columns and the headings seen). The fetch services record it on the `SourceRun` with `fmt` set on the failure entry (`services/source_formats.failure`); the list refreshes record their own run (`amfi_list`, `nse_list`) and a clean run after a good read. `source_formats.problems()` returns the standing ones (the newest run of each source), and the Alerts page lists them with a link to the format for an Admin. A key that is simply not in a good file stays an ordinary per-investment failure.
  - **Test this format** (`services/source_format_test.py`) parses a sample (upload, or a capped download through `adapters/http.py`) with the format as typed and stores nothing. The page is `catalogue/data-formats/` (Admin only, POST with CSRF); not configurable: new file structures such as nested or binary formats, authentication schemes, and Yahoo's split and dividend events.
- **Chart hover tooltips.** [Owner: hover the mouse over a line in all charts to see the values at that spot.] Every SVG line chart goes through `svgchart.line_chart` and `templates/investments/_chart.html` (Overview, each investment's value and price charts, Charts, Total Portfolio Value report), so each `<svg class="ch hv">` carries `data-hover`, a compact JSON of x positions, date labels and each line's formatted values at up to 150 sample days. `static/js/investments/chart-hover.js` (loaded by `base_investments.html`, no inline script) draws a dashed guide and a tooltip on pointer move or touch, and for keyboard users the chart is focusable (Left and Right arrows, Home, End, Escape). Without JS the chart is the same server-drawn SVG as before. The 22px trend sparklines in table cells are not line charts with axes and keep their text label; they have no hover tooltip.

- **D165. Whole price history, automatic 5-year pruning.** [Owner (2026-11): price history seemed to stop at 50 pages, about 10 years; older prices should be thinned to one a month automatically, except pinned dates.] **Cause of the limit:** not a page cap. The Catalogue price history page shows 50 rows a page (`catalogue.PAGE`) with no limit on pages; the data itself stopped at about 2,500 trading days because every fetch defaulted to `years=10` (`yahoo.fetch` set `period1` to today less 3,650 days; `fetch_prices`, `refetch_full`, the `--years` option of `fetch_prices` and the index add used the same default), and a new investment's history was fetched only that far back. mfapi.in was never limited (it returns all NAVs). **Back-fill:** a purchase back-dated before the stored history is still fetched back to the window start; only a window opening in the month of an oldest price already thinned by pruning is skipped, so a pruned instrument is not downloaded again each night. **Fix:** `yahoo.fetch` with no `since` and no `years` asks for `period1=0` (all the history Yahoo has); the callers pass no `years`. Fetched history is still clipped to the fetch window (the earliest purchase across profiles, PR #71), so only what is needed is stored. **Pruning is automatic:** `PriceHistorySettings.prune_live` and `prune_archive` default on and `years_kept` 5 (investments 0029 also switches on existing rows that still hold the old defaults; a row an Admin had explicitly left off before it ever ran cannot be told apart from the default and is switched on, so release text should say pruning is now automatic and can be turned off on the Admin page). Each night, as the last link of the cron chain and after a good backup, prices older than 5 years are thinned to the last stored trading day of each month, for every investment and index. **Safeguards kept:** pinned prices are never deleted (they live in `PinnedPrice`, and a stored price on a pinned trading date is kept too, even mid-month); an instrument with a missing required pin is left alone; no good backup today means no pruning; deletes are chunked with a time limit; the Admin page still shows a preview and can change the years (minimum 2) or switch pruning off. **Decision:** the first-run confirmation ("download a full archive and tick that you keep it") no longer applies. The owner asked for pruning to be automatic, so a gate that stops it until a person ticks a box would defeat that; the backup-first rule and the pin checks protect the data, and the full archive can still be downloaded from the Admin page at any time. The tick is kept as an optional note.

- **D166. Buy and Opening balance pick from Quill or the NSE / AMFI lists in one box.** [Owner (2026-11)] The Investment drop-down on Buy, Awaiting purchase and Opening balance (not Sell, Transfer, Switch, Dividend or an edit) is a searchable combobox, one per transaction card (`instrument-combobox.js`, built over the plain select, which stays in the page and is the whole control without JavaScript, with an "Add investment" link in `<noscript>`). Each card has a **Held** switch, on by default. On: focusing lists what the profile holds now with the units ("RELIANCE.NS · 120 u"); typing filters; no match says "Not held? Turn off Held to search everything". Off: nothing until two characters, then "In Madon.co.in" (the site name, shown as a heading band that cannot be picked) (the profile's usable investments, tagged sold or not held) and "From NSE / AMFI" (`instrument_search.search`, minus what already exists), and a last row "Not listed? Add it by hand" to the Add investment page. Both come from `investments_txn_lookup` (`/investments/transactions/lookup/?q=&held=0|1`, editor only, JSON, debounced and aborted like the Add investment picker). A pick from the NSE / AMFI group sets the hidden `new_source` and `new_code`; **nothing is created until the Buy is saved**, inside the same atomic save, and a page left open creates nothing. The server checks the code against `NseSecurity` / `AmfiScheme` and builds name, ISIN, plan and option from the stored record, never from posted text; a code not in the lists is a form error; an investment that already exists by code or ISIN is reused, and two cards picking the same new one make one. A last price line shows under the box after a pick (stored price or AMFI NAV; none for a new share), and Price is never prefilled. With the Admin lookup switch off, Held off searches only In Quill. Held units are worked out once per request and shared by all cards. The Held only tick box (D161) stays on the other forms.

- **D167. Buy shows a fund's plan and option everywhere; Date, Investment and Held share a row.** [Owner (2026-11)] A fund picked from the AMFI list shows "Regular Plan" and "Growth Option" in its result row, in the "New" line after picking and again after an error (the plan and option are read from the name when the stored ones are blank); a fund already in the site shows them from `AmfiScheme` by its code. The Date box is narrow, its help text is gone, and Date, the Investment box and the Held tick box sit on one row (wrapping on a phone) on every Buy, Awaiting purchase and Opening balance card.

- **D168. New Memo has one Firm then Matter picker.** [Owner (2026-11)] A Firm list (active firms), then that firm's active matters (grouped by Group when the firm has more than one), and a "Show archived firms and matters" tick box. Choosing a matter loads its defaults at once (`fees_memo_matter_defaults`, JSON). The defaults only fill boxes that are empty or still hold the previous matter's defaults, so anything typed is kept (the status line says how many boxes were kept). `?matter=` and `?firm=` links preselect. Without JavaScript the picker is a GET form that reloads the page. A finished memo still needs an active matter (a draft may use an archived one), and a posted `firm_id` must be the matter's firm.

## Round: fixed-width text boxes and Folio at 15 characters

Decision: no text box may grow with its content or push its neighbours onto a new line. `static/css/site.css` caps every `input[type=text]`, password, search, email and `select` at `28ch` by default. Long fields opt in to `48ch` by their name (names, addresses, notes, memo, payee, URL, subject, details, query), by type (`search`, `email`, `url`) or with the `wide` class; textareas are full width. Layouts that already size their own fields (the fees `.field-row` with `.short`, `.tiny`, `.grow`, the Buy combobox) set their own widths and are unaffected.

Folio is fixed at 15 characters (`15ch`). In the Investments forms the Folio select is `15ch` wide. In tables (Transactions, Dividends, the investment page lots and folios, the statement import review, corporate action rows) the cell carries `class="fo"` and `title="<full value>"`, so a longer folio is cut with an ellipsis and the full number appears when the cursor rests on it, the same way the Profile name does in the header.

## Round: one frame, one tab strip, one button set (design consistency)

- **Frame**: `.page` and `main` (72rem column, 20px/24px padding) are one shared rule in `design.css` for `body.look` and `body.ledger`, replacing identical copies in `look.css` and `ledger.css`. The `w-*` widths stay in `ledger.css`.
- **Tabs**: the Fees `.tabs` / `.stat-tabs` underline rules in `ledger.css` now also list Investments `nav.sub`, so there is one rule set; the pill look from `modern.css` is unchanged. Investments' own copy in `investments.css` is deleted. Home and Diary `.subnav`, and the smaller second row (`.sub-tabs`), keep their rules because they differ in padding and background.
- **Buttons**: three looks: primary (filled), secondary (outline), small/text. `btn p`, `btn q`, `btn sm`, `btn dng` are listed with `primary`, `secondary`, `small`, `danger` in `ledger.css`; Investments' duplicate button blocks are deleted. `button.linklike` is kept (it keeps the tap height, unlike `button.link`). Header Buy/Sell/Add stay bold text links, the refresh button stays borderless, and focus-visible outlines are untouched.
- No template markup or class names changed. Per-page CSS budget stays 143360 bytes; the heaviest page fell from 142446 to 140356.
- **D169. The portfolio Owner field is removed.** [Owner (2026-11)] `Portfolio.owner_name` was a free-text label that nothing read except its own Settings row. A portfolio is now just a name (usually the person's name). Migration investments 0034 drops the column; old backups that contain it restore with the key ignored. The Fee Register's `export_owner_name` is unrelated and stays.
